=N\int\log|x|\mathrm{d}(T_{\zeta}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)})(x)+o(N),

with
o(N)
uniform. Since
\mathrm{dist}(\zeta_{\mathbf{m}},\zeta|_{(q,1]}+\zeta([0,q])\,\delta_{q})<\varepsilon
,
|q-q_{\mathbf{m}}|<\varepsilon
and
\mathbf{m}\in[-1+\delta_{\varepsilon},1-\delta_{\varepsilon}]^{N}
, the maps
T_{\zeta}
and
T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}
are uniformly close on
[-1+\delta_{\varepsilon},1-\delta_{\varepsilon}]
, so the two pushed-forward measures are close in the Wasserstein sense. Moreover, by the second-order optimality condition (
B.27
), the support of
T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}
stays uniformly bounded away from
0
, which ensures that
\log|x|
is Lipschitz on the support. Hence there exists
C_{\varepsilon}>0
with
C_{\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
such that

(2.34)

\Bigl|\int\log|x|\mathrm{d}(T_{\zeta}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)})(x)-\int\log|x|\mathrm{d}(T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})})(x)\Bigr|\leq C_{\varepsilon}.

Moreover, by Lemma
C.3
and (
C.20
),

\int\log|x|\,\mathrm{d}(T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})})(x)=\int\log|T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(x)-\omega|\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}(x)+\frac{\Re(\omega)^{2}-\Im(\omega)^{2}}{2\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})},

where
\omega:=\omega_{T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})},\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}(0)
is the subordination function of
T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}
at time
\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})
and location
0
. Set

\omega^{\prime}:=\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})\int_{q_{\mathbf{m}}}^{1}\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}([0,t])\mathrm{d}t.

We show below that
\omega=\omega^{\prime}
. Since
\omega^{\prime}
is real, this will imply that
\omega\in\mathbb{R}
, and therefore the previous identity simplifies to

(2.35)

\int\log|x|\,\mathrm{d}(T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})})(x)=\int\log|T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(x)-\omega^{\prime}|\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}(x)+\frac{(\omega^{\prime})^{2}}{2\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}.

First,

(2.36)

G_{T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}(\omega^{\prime})=-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{i})}.

Set

\nu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}:=(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},\cdot))^{-1}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}.

Since
h_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},\cdot)=\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},\cdot)^{*}
, we have

(2.37)

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{i})}=\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}\left[\frac{1}{\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},\cdot)}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}[\partial_{xx}\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},\cdot)].

By Lemma
B.2
(since
\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}\in\mathcal{P}([q_{\mathbf{m}},1])
), we have

(2.38)

\partial_{xx}\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},x)=1-\int_{q_{\mathbf{m}}}^{1}\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(s,X_{s}))^{2}\mid X_{q_{\mathbf{m}}}=x]\mathrm{d}\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}(s).

Consider the Auffinger–Chen process

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}([0,t])\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\\
\mathrm{Law}(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},X_{q_{\mathbf{m}}}))=\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}.\end{cases}

By (
2.38
),

(2.39)

\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}[\partial_{xx}\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},\cdot)]=1-\int_{q_{\mathbf{m}}}^{1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(s,X_{s})\right)^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}(s).

By the optimality of
\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}
, we get from Lemma
B.6
(see (
B.26
)) that for every
s\in\mathrm{supp}(\zeta_{\mathbf{m}})
, one has

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\left(s,X_{s}\right)\right)^{2}\right]=s.

Hence, inserting this into (
2.39
) and using Stieltjes integration by parts, we get

\mathbb{E}_{\nu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}[\partial_{xx}\Phi_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},\cdot)]=\int_{q_{\mathbf{m}}}^{1}\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}([0,t])\mathrm{d}t.

Consequently, by (
2.37
) and (
2.36
),

G_{T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}(\omega^{\prime})=-\frac{\omega^{\prime}}{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})},

which gives

\omega^{\prime}+\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})G_{T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}(\omega^{\prime})=0.

By Lemma
C.2
, it remains to prove (recall (
C.13
)) that

(2.40)

\omega^{\prime}\in\overline{\Omega_{T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})},\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}},

or equivalently that

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{(T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(m_{i})-\omega^{\prime})^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}.

Notice that

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{(T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(m_{i})-\omega^{\prime})^{2}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{(\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{i}))^{2}}.

By the optimality of
\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}
, we have by Lemma
B.6
(see (
B.27
)) that

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{(\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{i}))^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})},

which proves (
2.40
). Thus, we have
\omega=\omega^{\prime}
. Substituting into (
2.35
) gives

(2.41)

\int\log|x|\,\mathrm{d}(T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})})(x)\\
=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{i})+\frac{(\omega^{\prime})^{2}}{2\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{i})+\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}{2}\!\left(\int_{q_{\mathbf{m}}}^{1}\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{\!2},

where we used
T_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(m)-\omega^{\prime}=\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m)
.
Since
|q_{\mathbf{m}}-q|\leq\varepsilon
,
\mathrm{dist}(\zeta_{\mathbf{m}},\zeta|_{(q,1]}+\zeta([0,q])\delta_{q})\leq\varepsilon
, and
|m_{i}|\leq 1-\delta_{\varepsilon}
, replacing
(q_{\mathbf{m}},\zeta_{\mathbf{m}})
by
(q,\zeta)
in (
2.41
) introduces an error of at most
C_{\varepsilon}
per coordinate (uniformly in
m_{i}\in[-1+\delta_{\varepsilon},1-\delta_{\varepsilon}]
). Combining with (
2.34
) and (
2.33
), we obtain

\log\mathbb{E}[|\det\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})|\mid\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf]\\
=\log\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})+{\frac{N}{2}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\left(\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}}+O_{\varepsilon}(N),

with
|O_{\varepsilon}(N)|\leq C_{\varepsilon}N
for some
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that
C_{\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
.
∎

2.5.
Upper bound on the complexity

We now prove the annealed upper bound on the complexity.

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
q\in(0,1)
and
u\in(0,1)
. Let
\zeta\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{2}(u,q)
. Let
f\in\mathbb{R}
. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and let
\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)
be the number of critical points
\mathbf{m}\in[-1,1]^{N}
of
F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}
in
\mathrm{SUSY}_{1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
such that
\frac{1}{N}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})\in(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)
.

Then,

(2.42)

\limsup_{\varepsilon\to 0}\limsup_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]\leq u\left(\Phi_{\zeta}(0,0)-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t-f\right).

Proof.

We write
C_{\varepsilon}
for a positive constant satisfying
C_{\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
,
and
O_{\varepsilon}(N)
for a quantity bounded in absolute value by
C_{\varepsilon}N
.

We first derive the analogue of (
2.43
) in the mixed and pure cases.

Mixed case.

Recall from Lemma
2.4
that

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]=N\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\int_{(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\mid\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf^{\prime}]\\
\times\varphi_{(\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}),F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))}(0,Nf^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{SUSY}_{1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)}(\mathbf{m})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}\mathrm{d}f^{\prime}.

By Lemma
2.7
, for every
\mathbf{m}\in[-1,1]^{N}
such that
q_{\mathbf{m}}=\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{m}\|^{2}\in(q-\varepsilon,q+\varepsilon)
and
\mathrm{dist}(\zeta_{\mathbf{m}},\zeta|_{(q,1]}+\zeta([0,q])\delta_{q})<\varepsilon
,

\log\mathbb{E}[|\det\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})|\mid\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf^{\prime}]\\
\leq\log\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})+{\frac{N}{2}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\left(\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}}+C_{\varepsilon}N.

Moreover, by Lemma
2.5
(Equation (
2.8
)), if in addition
\mathbf{m}\in\mathrm{SUSY}_{1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
,

\log\varphi_{(\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}),F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))}(0,Nf^{\prime})\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}N\xi(q)u^{2}-\frac{1}{2}N\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\left(\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})-u\xi^{\prime}(q)\mathbf{m}\|^{2}-\frac{N}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))\\
-u\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})+N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)+Nf^{\prime}\right)+N\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{2q\xi^{\prime}(q)}+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Combining the two above displays, noticing that the two terms
\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)(\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t)^{2}
cancel each other, and using that
f^{\prime}\in(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)
, we deduce that

(2.43)

\log\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]\leq\frac{1}{2}N\xi(q)u^{2}-uN\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)-Nuf-\frac{N}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))\\
+\log\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})\,e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})-u\xi^{\prime}(q)\mathbf{m}\|^{2}}\\
\times\mathbf{1}_{\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{m}\|^{2}\in(q-\varepsilon,q+\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Pure case.

By Lemma
2.4
, (
2.4
),

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]=\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\,\middle|\,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0\right]\varphi_{\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})}(0)\\
\times\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{SUSY}_{1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)}(\mathbf{m})\,\mathbf{1}_{(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)}\!\left(\frac{1}{N}\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m})\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}.

Fix
\mathbf{m}\in\mathrm{SUSY}_{1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
and set

f_{\mathbf{m}}:=\frac{1}{N}\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m}),\qquad T(\mathbf{m}):=\frac{1}{p}\langle\mathbf{m},k_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\rangle-\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q).

By (
2.6
), on the event

\{\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0\}
one has

F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf_{\mathbf{m}}
. Therefore

\mathbb{E}\!\left[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\,\middle|\,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}\!\left[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\,\middle|\,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,\,F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf_{\mathbf{m}}\right].

Hence, on the support of the indicator

\mathbf{1}_{(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)}(N^{-1}\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))
, Lemma
2.7
yields

\log\mathbb{E}\!\left[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\,\middle|\,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0\right]\\
\leq\log\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})+\frac{N}{2}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\left(\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Comparing the exact identity

F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=\frac{1}{p}\langle\mathbf{m},\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})\rangle+\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m})

with (
2.27
), we obtain

\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=T(\mathbf{m})+O_{\varepsilon}(N)

uniformly over
\mathbf{m}\in\mathrm{SUSY}_{1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
. Hence, on the support of

\mathbf{1}_{(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)}(N^{-1}\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))
,

u\bigl(T(\mathbf{m})-Nf\bigr)=O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Therefore, by Lemma
2.6
, (
2.22
), and the bound

\frac{p-1}{p}\leq 1
, we get

\log\varphi_{\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})}(0)\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}N\xi(q)u^{2}-\frac{1}{2}N\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\left(\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})-u\xi^{\prime}(q)\mathbf{m}\|^{2}-\frac{N}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))\\
+\frac{N}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)q}\left(\frac{1}{N}\langle\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m}),\mathbf{m}\rangle-\xi^{\prime}(q)\int_{0}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}-u\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})+N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)+Nf\right)+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Combining the last two displays and dropping the indicator

\mathbf{1}_{(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)}(N^{-1}\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))
, we obtain again
(
2.43
).

Notice that

\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})-u\xi^{\prime}(q)\mathbf{m}\|^{2}=\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}-2u\xi^{\prime}(q)\langle\mathbf{m},\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\rangle+\xi^{\prime}(q)^{2}u^{2}\|\mathbf{m}\|^{2}.

Hence, plugging this into (
2.43
) and dropping the constraint that
\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{m}\|^{2}\in(q-\varepsilon,q+\varepsilon)
, we deduce that

(2.44)

\log\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]\leq\frac{1}{2}N\xi(q)u^{2}-uN\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)-Nuf-\frac{N}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))-\frac{1}{2}N\xi^{\prime}(q)qu^{2}\\
+\log\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Let
g:(-1,1)\to\mathbb{R}
be strictly convex and differentiable and such that
\lim_{x\to-1}g^{\prime}(x)=-\infty
and
\lim_{x\to 1}g^{\prime}(x)=\infty
. Recall that the Legendre transform of
g
is given for every
y\in\mathbb{R}
by

g^{*}(y)=\sup_{x\in(-1,1)}(yx-g(x)).

Moreover, the optimal
x
is unique and given by
x=(g^{\prime})^{-1}(y)
(notice that
g^{\prime}
is strictly monotonic and surjective). Hence,
y=g^{\prime}(x)
and

(2.45)

g(x)-xg^{\prime}(x)=-g^{*}(g^{\prime}(x)).

Applying this to
g=h_{\zeta}(q,\cdot)
(which satisfies the above assumption by Lemma
B.5
), we obtain

\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}\\
=\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})e^{u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,\cdot))^{*}(\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m))-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}.

Performing the change of variables
y=\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)
yields

(2.46)

\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}\\
=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{uh_{\zeta}(q,\cdot)^{*}(y)-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}y^{2}}\mathrm{d}y\right)^{N}.

Hence,

\log\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}-\frac{N}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))\\
=N\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{N}(0,\xi^{\prime}(q))}[e^{u\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X)}].

Recall that for every
x\in\mathbb{R}
,

\Phi_{\zeta}(0,x)=\frac{1}{u}\log\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\xi^{\prime}(q))}\Bigl[e^{u\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X+x)}\Bigr].

Hence, we find

(2.47)

\log\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}-\frac{N}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))\\
=uN\Phi_{\zeta}(0,0).

Moreover, notice that

u(\xi(q)-\xi^{\prime}(q)q)=-\int_{0}^{q}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t,

since
\zeta([0,q))=\zeta(\{0\})=u
. Recalling that
\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{q}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t
, we deduce that

\frac{1}{2}u^{2}(\xi(q)-\xi^{\prime}(q)q)-u\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)=-\frac{u}{2}\int_{0}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t.

Inserting this into (
2.47
), we obtain

(2.48)

\frac{1}{2}N\xi(q)u^{2}-uN\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)-Nuf-\frac{N}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))-\frac{1}{2}N\xi^{\prime}(q)qu^{2}\\
+\log\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}\\
=Nu\left(\Phi_{\zeta}(0,0)-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t-f\right).

By (
2.44
), this proves the lemma.
∎

2.6.
Optimality consequences

In this subsection, we derive necessary conditions satisfied by the optimizer
\zeta
of the Parisi functional over
\mathrm{Prefix}_{2}(u,q)
and by the critical point
(u,q)
of the complexity functional. These conditions, expressed in terms of the Auffinger–Chen process, are needed in the proof of the lower bound.

Lemma 2.9
.

Let
f\in\mathbb{R}
,
q\in(0,1)
and
u\in(0,1)
. Let
\zeta\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{2}(u,q)
satisfy the assumptions of Proposition
2.2
. Let
(X_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
be the Auffinger–Chen process

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\\
X_{0}=0.\end{cases}

For every
s\in[0,1]
, set

H_{\zeta}(s):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{1}\xi^{\prime\prime}(r)\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(r,X_{r}))^{2}]-r\Bigr)\mathrm{d}r.

Then, the following hold:

(i)

(2.49)

-\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})]+\int_{0}^{q}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{2}\int_{q}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t=-f,

(ii)

(2.50)

\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q}))^{2}]=q,

(iii)

(2.51)

\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\cap[q,1]\subset\underset{[q,1]}{\mathrm{arg\,min}}(H_{\zeta}).

Proof.

We first record the first-variation formula for
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}
. By Remark
B.1
and Lemma
B.6
(ii)
with
q=0
and
\mu=\delta_{0}
, for every finite signed measure
\eta
on
[0,1]
with
\eta([0,1])=0
,

D\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta)[\eta]=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\xi^{\prime\prime}(s)\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(s,X_{s}))^{2}]-s\Bigr)\eta([0,s])\,\mathrm{d}s=\int_{0}^{1}H_{\zeta}(s)\,\mathrm{d}\eta(s),

where the second equality is Fubini’s theorem.

Set

\lambda:=\frac{1}{1-u}\,\zeta|_{[q,1]}\in\mathcal{P}([q,1]),\qquad\zeta=u\delta_{0}+(1-u)\lambda.

Since
\zeta\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{2}(u,q)
, we have
q\in\mathrm{supp}(\lambda)
.

Step 1: proof of (
2.51
).

Let
\lambda^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}([q,1])
with
q\in\mathrm{supp}(\lambda^{\prime})
, and define

\lambda_{\theta}:=(1-\theta)\lambda+\theta\lambda^{\prime},\qquad\zeta_{\theta}:=u\delta_{0}+(1-u)\lambda_{\theta},\qquad\theta\in[0,1].

Then
\zeta_{\theta}\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{2}(u,q)
. Since
\zeta
minimizes
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}
over
\mathrm{Prefix}_{2}(u,q)
, the right derivative at
\theta=0
is nonnegative:

0\leq\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\theta}\bigg|_{\theta=0^{+}}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta_{\theta})=(1-u)\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}(s)\,\mathrm{d}(\lambda^{\prime}-\lambda)(s).

Hence

\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\leq\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda^{\prime}

for every
\lambda^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}([q,1])
with
q\in\mathrm{supp}(\lambda^{\prime})
.

Now let
\nu\in\mathcal{P}([q,1])
be arbitrary and set

\nu_{\varepsilon}:=(1-\varepsilon)\nu+\varepsilon\delta_{q}.

Then
q\in\mathrm{supp}(\nu_{\varepsilon})
, so

\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\leq\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{\varepsilon}.

By Lemma
B.1
(iv)
, the map

s\mapsto\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(s,X_{s}))^{2}]
is continuous, hence
H_{\zeta}
is continuous. Letting
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
, we obtain

\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda\leq\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}\,\mathrm{d}\nu\qquad\text{for every }\nu\in\mathcal{P}([q,1]).

Therefore
\lambda
minimizes the linear functional

\nu\mapsto\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}\,\mathrm{d}\nu
over
\mathcal{P}([q,1])
, and hence

\mathrm{supp}(\lambda)\subset\underset{[q,1]}{\mathrm{arg\,min}}(H_{\zeta}).

Since
\mathrm{supp}(\lambda)=\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\cap[q,1]
, this is exactly
(
2.51
).

Step 2: proof of (
2.50
).

Set

\mu:=\mathrm{Law}\bigl(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})\bigr),\qquad\zeta_{\star}:=\zeta|_{(q,1]}+\zeta([0,q])\delta_{q}\in\mathcal{P}([q,1]).

For every
t\in[q,1]
, one has
\zeta_{\star}([0,t])=\zeta([0,t])
, hence

\Phi_{\zeta_{\star}}(t,\cdot)=\Phi_{\zeta}(t,\cdot),\qquad t\in[q,1].

Let
(X_{t}^{\mu})_{t\in[q,1]}
be the Auffinger–Chen process from
(
B.21
) associated with
(\mu,\zeta_{\star})
.
Because

\mu=\mathrm{Law}(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q}))\qquad\text{and}\qquad\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\star}}(q,\cdot)=\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,\cdot),

we may choose
X_{q}^{\mu}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}X_{q}
. The SDEs on
[q,1]
then coincide, so

(X_{t}^{\mu})_{t\in[q,1]}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}(X_{t})_{t\in[q,1]}.

Consequently, for every
s\in[q,1]
,

H_{\zeta_{\star}}^{\mu}(s)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{1}\xi^{\prime\prime}(r)\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\star}}(r,X_{r}^{\mu}))^{2}]-r\Bigr)\mathrm{d}r=H_{\zeta}(s).

By (
2.51
), every point of
\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\cap[q,1]
minimizes

H_{\zeta}
on
[q,1]
. Since
q\in\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)
, we also have

H_{\zeta}(q)=\min_{[q,1]}H_{\zeta}.

Therefore every point of

\mathrm{supp}(\zeta_{\star})=\{q\}\cup(\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\cap(q,1])

minimizes
H_{\zeta_{\star}}^{\mu}
on
[q,1]
, and hence minimizes its extension

\hat{H}_{\zeta_{\star}}^{\mu}
from (
B.24
) on
[0,1]
.
By Lemma
B.6
(iii)
,

\zeta_{\star}
is the unique minimizer of
\mathrm{TAP}(\mu,\cdot)
on
\mathcal{P}([q,1])
.
Applying Lemma
B.6
(iv)
at the point

q\in\mathrm{supp}(\zeta_{\star})
, we get

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\star}}(q,X_{q}^{\mu}))^{2}\bigr]=q.

Since
\Phi_{\zeta_{\star}}(q,\cdot)=\Phi_{\zeta}(q,\cdot)
and
X_{q}^{\mu}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}X_{q}
,
this is exactly (
2.50
).

Step 3: proof of (
2.49
).

For
v
close to
u
, define

\zeta_{v}:=v\delta_{0}+(1-v)\lambda.

Because
q\in\mathrm{supp}(\lambda)
, we have
\zeta_{v}\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{2}(v,q)
. Set

g(v):=v\bigl(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta_{v})-f\bigr).

By definition of
\mathcal{P}(v;q)
,

\mathcal{C}_{f}(v;q)=v\bigl(\mathcal{P}(v;q)-f\bigr)\leq g(v),

and equality holds at
v=u
, because
\zeta_{u}=\zeta
and

\zeta
attains the infimum defining
\mathcal{P}(u;q)
.
Hence the function
g-\mathcal{C}_{f}(\cdot;q)
has a local minimum
0
at
u
.
Since
u
is a critical point of
v\mapsto\mathcal{C}_{f}(v;q)
, we obtain

0=(g-\mathcal{C}_{f}(\cdot;q))^{\prime}(u)=g^{\prime}(u).

Using the first-variation formula above and the fact that

\mathrm{supp}(\lambda)\subset\mathrm{argmin}_{[q,1]}H_{\zeta}
, we obtain

0=g^{\prime}(u)=\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta)-f+u\,D\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta)[\delta_{0}-\lambda]=\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta)-f+u\bigl(H_{\zeta}(0)-H_{\zeta}(q)\bigr).

Now Lemma
B.1
(i)
gives

\mathrm{d}\Phi_{\zeta}(t,X_{t})=\frac{1}{2}\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\zeta([0,t])\bigl(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(t,X_{t})\bigr)^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)}\,\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(t,X_{t})\,\mathrm{d}B_{t}.

Taking expectations and integrating from
0
to
q
,

\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})]=\Phi_{\zeta}(0,0)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{q}\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\zeta([0,t])\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(t,X_{t}))^{2}\bigr]\mathrm{d}t.

Since
\zeta([0,t])=u
for
t<q
, this implies

\Phi_{\zeta}(0,0)+u\bigl(H_{\zeta}(0)-H_{\zeta}(q)\bigr)=\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})]-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{q}t\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t.

Recalling that

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta)=\Phi_{\zeta}(0,0)-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}t\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t,

we deduce

\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta)+u\bigl(H_{\zeta}(0)-H_{\zeta}(q)\bigr)=\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})]-\int_{0}^{q}t\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t-\frac{1}{2}\int_{q}^{1}t\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t.

Substituting this into the identity
0=g^{\prime}(u)
yields

-\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})]+\int_{0}^{q}t\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{2}\int_{q}^{1}t\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t=-f,

which is (
2.49
).
∎

2.7.
Lower bound on the complexity

In this subsection, we prove the matching lower bound on the annealed complexity, completing the proof of Proposition
2.2
. The argument combines the Kac–Rice representation with a reduction to a “good” event, and uses the optimality conditions from the previous subsection to verify that this event has full probability.

Proof of Proposition
2.2
.

By Lemma
2.8
, it remains to prove the lower bound. Let
\zeta\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{2}(u,q)
. We write
C_{\varepsilon}
for a positive constant satisfying
C_{\varepsilon}\to 0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
,
and
O_{\varepsilon}(N)
for a quantity bounded in absolute value by
C_{\varepsilon}N
.

Set

\zeta_{\star}:=\zeta|_{(q,1]}+\zeta([0,q])\,\delta_{q}.

Step 1: reduction to a good event

Set
\delta_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Let
\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)
denote the set of
\mathbf{m}\in[-1+\delta_{\varepsilon},1-\delta_{\varepsilon}]^{N}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{m}\|^{2}\in[q-\varepsilon,\,q+\varepsilon],

\displaystyle\mathrm{dist}(\zeta_{\mathbf{m}},\,\zeta_{\star})\leq\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})\,m_{i}-\xi^{\prime}(q)\int_{0}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\right|\leq\varepsilon,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})\,m_{i}\bigr)+\int_{0}^{q}t\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t+\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{q}^{1}t\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\in[-f-\varepsilon,-f+\varepsilon].

We claim that

(2.52)

\log\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]\geq\frac{1}{2}N\xi(q)u^{2}-uN\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)-Nuf-\frac{N}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))-\frac{1}{2}N\xi^{\prime}(q)qu^{2}\\
+\log\int\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)}(\mathbf{m})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}\\
+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

We prove this separately in the mixed and pure cases.

Mixed case.

By Lemma
2.4
,

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]=N\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\int_{(f-\varepsilon,\,f+\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}\bigl[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\mid\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,\,F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf^{\prime}\bigr]\\
\times\,\varphi_{(\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}),\,F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))}(0,Nf^{\prime})\,\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{SUSY}_{1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)}(\mathbf{m})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}\,\mathrm{d}f^{\prime}.

Since
\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)\subset\mathrm{SUSY}_{1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
,

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]\geq N\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\int_{(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)}\mathbb{E}[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\mid\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf^{\prime}]\\
\times\varphi_{(\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}),F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))}(0,Nf^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)}(\mathbf{m})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}\mathrm{d}f^{\prime}.

By Lemma
2.7
, for every
\mathbf{m}\in\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)
,

\log\mathbb{E}[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\mid\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf^{\prime}]\\
\geq\log\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})+{\frac{N}{2}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\left(\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}}+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Moreover, by Lemma
2.5
, there exists
C>0
such that for every
\mathbf{m}\in\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)
,

\log\varphi_{(\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}),F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))}(0,Nf^{\prime})\\
=\frac{1}{2}N\xi(q)u^{2}-\frac{1}{2}N\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\left(\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})-u\xi^{\prime}(q)\mathbf{m}\|^{2}-\frac{N}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))\\
-u\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})+N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)+Nf^{\prime}\right)+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Inserting the last two displays into the previous lower bound, and using that
f^{\prime}\in(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)
, yields (
2.52
).

Pure case.

By Lemma
2.4
, (
2.4
),

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]=\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\,\middle|\,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0\right]\varphi_{\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})}(0)\\
\times\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{SUSY}_{1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)}(\mathbf{m})\,\mathbf{1}_{(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)}\!\left(\frac{1}{N}\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m})\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}.

For
\mathbf{m}\in\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)
, set

T(\mathbf{m}):=\frac{1}{p}\langle\mathbf{m},k_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\rangle-\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q).

The same algebra as in (
2.32
) gives

T(\mathbf{m})=-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i}\bigr)-N\int_{0}^{q}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t-\frac{N}{2}\int_{q}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\\
+\frac{p-1}{p}\!\left(N\xi^{\prime}(q)\int_{0}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t-\langle\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m}),\mathbf{m}\rangle\right).

By the definition of
\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)
, this implies

T(\mathbf{m})=Nf+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Comparing the exact identity

F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=\frac{1}{p}\langle\mathbf{m},\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})\rangle+\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m})

with (
2.27
), we obtain

\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=T(\mathbf{m})+O_{\varepsilon}(N)=Nf+O_{\varepsilon}(N)

uniformly on
\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)
. After decreasing
\varepsilon
if necessary (and relabelling it), we may therefore assume that

\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)\subset\left\{\mathbf{m}:\frac{1}{N}\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m})\in(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)\right\}.

Hence

\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]\geq\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\,\middle|\,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0\right]\varphi_{\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})}\!(0)\,\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)}(\mathbf{m})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}.

Now fix
\mathbf{m}\in\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)
and set

f_{\mathbf{m}}:=\frac{1}{N}\mathcal{R}^{\rm ex}_{\zeta}(\mathbf{m}).

By (
2.6
), on the event

\{\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0\}
one has

F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf_{\mathbf{m}}
. Therefore

\mathbb{E}\!\left[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\,\middle|\,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0\right]\\
=\mathbb{E}\!\left[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\,\middle|\,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,\,F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf_{\mathbf{m}}\right].

Hence, Lemma
2.7
implies

\log\mathbb{E}\!\left[|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|\,\middle|\,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0\right]\\
\geq\log\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})+\frac{N}{2}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\left(\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Moreover,
\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)
implies (
2.23
) and (
2.24
), so by
Lemma
2.6
, (
2.25
), and
T(\mathbf{m})=Nf+O_{\varepsilon}(N)
,

\log\varphi_{\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})}(0)\\
=\frac{1}{2}N\xi(q)u^{2}-\frac{1}{2}N\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\left(\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})-u\xi^{\prime}(q)\mathbf{m}\|^{2}-\frac{N}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))\\
-u\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})+N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)+Nf\right)+O_{\varepsilon}(N).

Inserting the last two displays into the pure Kac–Rice lower bound above yields
(
2.52
).

Step 2: checking the constraints asymptotically

Consider the probability measure
\mu
on
[-1,1]
with density

\mathrm{d}\mu(m)\propto\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m)e^{-u(h_{\zeta}(q,m)-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)m)-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)^{2}}\mathrm{d}m.

Let
\nu:=(\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m))\#\mu
. Observe that by the Legendre transform identity (
2.45
),
\nu
is the probability measure on
\mathbb{R}
with density

\frac{\mathrm{d}\nu}{\mathrm{d}y}(y)\propto e^{-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}y^{2}+u\Phi_{\zeta}(q,y)}.

Hence, by Lemma
B.3
, we get that

\nu=\mathrm{Law}(X_{q}),

where
X_{t}
is the Auffinger–Chen process

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\\
X_{0}=0.\end{cases}

\bullet
First,

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[m^{2}]=\mathbb{E}_{\nu}[\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,\cdot)^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})^{2}].

By Lemma
2.9
, (see (
2.50
)),

\mathbb{E}[\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})^{2}]=q.

Thus,

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[m^{2}]=q.

\bullet
One has

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[m\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)]=\mathbb{E}[\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})X_{q}]=\xi^{\prime}(q)\int_{0}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t,

where we have used (
B.18
) in Lemma
B.4
with
s=0
in the last equality.

\bullet
By the Legendre transform identity (
2.45
), notice that

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[h_{\zeta}(q,m)-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)m]=-\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})].

By Lemma
2.9
, Equation (
2.49
),

-\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{\zeta}(q,X_{q})]+\int_{0}^{q}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{2}\int_{q}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t=-f.

Therefore,

\mathbb{E}_{\mu}[h_{\zeta}(q,m)-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)m]+\int_{0}^{q}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{2}\int_{q}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t=-f.

\bullet
By Lemma
B.6
, to prove that
\zeta_{\star}
is the unique minimizer of
\mathrm{TAP}(\mu,\cdot)
, it is enough to check that for every
\zeta^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}([q,1])
,

(2.53)

\int_{q}^{1}H^{\mu}_{\zeta_{\star}}(s)\mathrm{d}\zeta^{\prime}(s)\geq\int_{q}^{1}H^{\mu}_{\zeta_{\star}}(s)\mathrm{d}\zeta_{\star}(s),

where for every
s\in[q,1]
,

H^{\mu}_{\zeta_{\star}}(s)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{1}\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\star}}(t,X_{t}^{\mu}))^{2}]-t\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t,

and
(X_{t}^{\mu})_{t\in[q,1]}
is the Auffinger–Chen process

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}X_{t}^{\mu}=\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta_{\star}([0,t])\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\star}}(t,X^{\mu}_{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)}\mathrm{d}B_{t},\\
\mathrm{Law}(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta_{\star}}(q,X^{\mu}_{q}))=\mu.\end{cases}

Since
\zeta_{\star}([0,t])=\zeta([0,t])
for every
t\in[q,1]
, the Parisi PDE on
[q,1]
is the same for
\zeta_{\star}
and
\zeta
. Hence

\Phi_{\zeta_{\star}}(t,\cdot)=\Phi_{\zeta}(t,\cdot)\qquad\text{for every }t\in[q,1].

Moreover, by the construction of
\mu
and the identity
\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\cdot)=(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,\cdot))^{-1}
, we have

(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,\cdot))^{-1}\#\mu=\mathrm{Law}(X_{q}).

Therefore,
(X_{t}^{\mu})_{t\in[q,1]}
has the same distribution as
(X_{t})_{t\in[q,1]}
, and so

H^{\mu}_{\zeta_{\star}}(s)=H_{\zeta}(s):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{1}\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(t,X_{t}))^{2}]-t\Bigr)\mathrm{d}t,\qquad s\in[q,1].

By Lemma
2.9
, Equation (
2.51
), every point of
\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\cap[q,1]
minimizes
H_{\zeta}
on
[q,1]
. Since
\zeta\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{2}(u,q)
, we have
q\in\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)
, hence

H_{\zeta}(q)=\min_{[q,1]}H_{\zeta}.

It follows that every point of

\mathrm{supp}(\zeta_{\star})=\{q\}\cup(\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\cap(q,1])

also minimizes
H_{\zeta}
on
[q,1]
. Consequently,

\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}(s)\mathrm{d}\zeta_{\star}(s)=H_{\zeta}(q)=\min_{[q,1]}H_{\zeta}.

Therefore, for every probability measure
\zeta^{\prime}
on
[q,1]
,

\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}(s)\mathrm{d}\zeta^{\prime}(s)\geq\min_{[q,1]}H_{\zeta}=\int_{q}^{1}H_{\zeta}(s)\mathrm{d}\zeta_{\star}(s).

This proves (
2.53
), and thus
\zeta_{\star}
is the unique minimizer of
\mathrm{TAP}(\mu,\cdot)
by strict convexity.

Step 3: control on the good event under the truncated product law

Set

w_{\zeta}(m):=\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m)e^{-u(h_{\zeta}(q,m)-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)m)-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)^{2}}

and, for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

Z_{\varepsilon}:=\int_{-1+\delta_{\varepsilon}}^{1-\delta_{\varepsilon}}w_{\zeta}(m)\,\mathrm{d}m,\qquad\mathrm{d}\mu_{\varepsilon}(m):=\frac{\mathbf{1}_{[-1+\delta_{\varepsilon},1-\delta_{\varepsilon}]}(m)\,w_{\zeta}(m)}{Z_{\varepsilon}}\,\mathrm{d}m.

Since
w_{\zeta}\in L^{1}([-1,1])
(this follows from the change of variables
m=\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,y)
, which identifies
w_{\zeta}\,\mathrm{d}m
with the density of
\mathrm{Law}(X_{q})
up to a normalizing constant; see Lemma
B.3
), we have
Z_{\varepsilon}\uparrow Z_{0}:=\int_{-1}^{1}w_{\zeta}(m)\,\mathrm{d}m
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
, and
\mu_{\varepsilon}\Rightarrow\mu
weakly.

Let

q_{\varepsilon}:=\int m^{2}\,\mu_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}m)

and let
\zeta_{\star,\varepsilon}
be the unique minimizer of
\mathrm{TAP}(\mu_{\varepsilon},\cdot)
.
By Step 2, the functions

m\mapsto m^{2},\qquad m\mapsto m\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m),\qquad m\mapsto h_{\zeta}(q,m)-m\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)

are
\mu
-integrable, so their expectations under
\mu_{\varepsilon}
converge to the corresponding target values appearing in the definition of
\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)
. Moreover, by Lemma
B.9
, since
q_{\varepsilon}\to q
and
\mu_{\varepsilon}\Rightarrow\mu
, we have

\mathrm{dist}(\zeta_{\star,\varepsilon},\zeta_{\star})\xrightarrow[\varepsilon\to 0]{}0.

After decreasing
\varepsilon
if necessary (and relabelling it), we may therefore assume that all four discrepancies are at most
\varepsilon/4
.

Now let
m_{1},\ldots,m_{N}
be i.i.d. with law
\mu_{\varepsilon}
. By the law of large numbers, with probability tending to
1
as
N\to\infty
,

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}m_{i}^{2}-q_{\varepsilon}\right|<\varepsilon/4,

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}m_{i}\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\int m\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)\,\mu_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}m)\right|<\varepsilon/4,

and

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-m_{i}\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})\bigr)-\int\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q,m)-m\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)\bigr)\,\mu_{\varepsilon}(\mathrm{d}m)\right|<\varepsilon/4.

Furthermore, by the i.i.d. part of Lemma
B.9
applied to
\mu_{\varepsilon}
,

\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes N}\!\left(\mathrm{dist}(\zeta_{\mathbf{m}},\zeta_{\star,\varepsilon})>\varepsilon/4\right)\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0.

Using the bounds on the expectations and the triangle inequality, we conclude that

\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes N}(\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)^{c})\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0.

Step 4: conclusion

For every
N
and every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\int_{[-1+\delta_{\varepsilon},1-\delta_{\varepsilon}]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)}(\mathbf{m})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}\\
=Z_{\varepsilon}^{N}\,\mu_{\varepsilon}^{\otimes N}(\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)).

Hence, by Step 3,

\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\int_{[-1+\delta_{\varepsilon},1-\delta_{\varepsilon}]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})\\
\times e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)}(\mathbf{m})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}\geq\log Z_{\varepsilon}.

Since
Z_{\varepsilon}\uparrow Z_{0}
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
, we get

\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\int_{[-1+\delta_{\varepsilon},1-\delta_{\varepsilon}]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})\\
\times e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathrm{Good}(\varepsilon)}(\mathbf{m})\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}\geq\log Z_{0}.

But

Z_{0}=\int_{-1}^{1}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m)e^{-u(h_{\zeta}(q,m)-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)m)-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m)^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}m,

so

\log Z_{0}=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})\\
\times e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}.

Thus, by (
2.52
),

\liminf_{\varepsilon\to 0}\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q)]\geq\frac{1}{2}\xi(q)u^{2}-u\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q)-uf-\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi\xi^{\prime}(q))-\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}(q)qu^{2}\\
+\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\int_{[-1,1]^{N}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\partial_{mm}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})e^{-u\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})m_{i})-\frac{1}{2\xi^{\prime}(q)}\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q,\mathbf{m})\|^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{m}.

Inserting (
2.48
), this concludes the proof of the proposition.
∎

3.
The conditional annealed computation

The aim of the section is to perform conditional annealed computations of the complexity of the TAP free energy. We consider a hierarchy of ancestors and count the number of critical points in the band orthogonal to the last ancestor, conditionally on all the ancestors being critical points of the TAP free energy and conditionally on their free energy level. We will extend the computations of Section
2
. We assume in this section that
\xi
is a mixed model, i.e., at least two of the
coefficients
\beta_{p}^{2}
are nonzero.

3.1.
Statement of the conditional annealed estimate

In this subsection, we state the main result of Section
3
: the conditional annealed estimate for the TAP complexity. As in Section
2
, we first restrict our attention to “SUSY” states, imposing conditions on the hierarchy
(\mathbf{m}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{m}^{(n)})
that ensure the Ward identities are satisfied.

Definition 3.1

(SUSY hierarchy of states)
.

Fix
n\geq 1
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Set
\mathbf{m}^{(0)}=0
. Let
(u_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}
and

(q_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}
be strictly increasing sequences in
(0,1)
, and let

\zeta\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{n+1}\bigl((u_{i}),(q_{i})\bigr)
as in Definition
1.2
.
We define
\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
as the set of

(\mathbf{m}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{m}^{(n)})\in([-1+\delta_{\varepsilon},1-\delta_{\varepsilon}]^{N})^{n}
where
\delta_{\varepsilon}:=\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
and such that, for every
k\in[n]

and
j\in\{1,\ldots,k-1\}
,

\left|\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\|^{2}-q_{k}\right|\leq\varepsilon\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left|\frac{1}{N}\langle\mathbf{m}^{(k)}-\mathbf{m}^{(k-1)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle\right|\leq\varepsilon,

and, for every
i,k\in[n]
,

(3.1)

\left|\frac{1}{N}\langle\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},\mathbf{m}^{(k)}),\mathbf{m}^{(i)}\rangle-\mathbb{E}\bigl[X_{q_{k}}\,\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{i},X_{q_{i}})\bigr]\right|\leq\varepsilon,

and, for every
k\in[n]
,

\mathrm{dist}\!\bigl(\zeta_{\mathbf{m}^{(k)}},\,\zeta|_{(q_{k},1]}+\zeta([0,q_{k}])\,\delta_{q_{k}}\bigr)\leq\varepsilon.

Note that the SUSY assumption is only (
3.1
). It would be interesting to know if one can remove it.

Next, we make some assumptions on the ancestors
only
. Roughly speaking, we want the empirical measure of the coordinates of the ancestor
\mathbf{m}^{(k)}
to be distributed approximately according to the law of
\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{k},X_{q_{k}})
where
(X_{t})
is the Auffinger–Chen process and
q_{k}:=\frac{1}{N}\|\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\|^{2}
. In fact, we only need these two distributions to agree on a
finite number
of linear statistics.

Definition 3.2

(Ancestors matching the target law)
.

Fix
n\geq 2
. Let
\zeta
be a probability measure on
[0,1]
with

(n+1)
-atom prefix
\{0,q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\}
as in Definition
1.2
;
the tail
\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\cap(q_{n},1]
may be nonempty.
Let
(X_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
be the Auffinger–Chen process (
B.6
),
and write
U:=\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},X_{q_{n-1}})
.
Define
\mathrm{Test}:=\{\chi_{1},\ldots,\chi_{5}\}
, where each
\chi_{i}:[-1,1]\to\mathbb{R}
is given by

(3.2)

\displaystyle\chi_{1}(m)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\!\left[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{n},X_{q_{n}}))^{2}\;\middle|\;U=m\right],

(3.3)

\displaystyle\chi_{2}(m)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{n},X_{q_{n}})\,X_{q_{n}}\;\middle|\;U=m\right],

(3.4)

\displaystyle\chi_{3}(m)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}[\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{n},X_{q_{n}})\mid U=m]=m,

(3.5)

\displaystyle\chi_{4}(m)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\!\left[X_{q_{n}}\;\middle|\;U=m\right],

(3.6)

\displaystyle\chi_{5}(m)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{n},X_{q_{n}})\;\middle|\;U=m\right].

For
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, let
\delta_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}
and define
\mathrm{LawMatch}_{n-1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
as the set of

(\mathbf{m}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)})\in([-1+\delta_{\varepsilon},1-\delta_{\varepsilon}]^{N})^{n-1}
such that, for every

\chi\in\mathrm{Test}
and
k\in[n-1]
,

(3.7)

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\chi(m_{i}^{(n-1)})\,m_{i}^{(k)}-\mathbb{E}\!\left[\chi(U)\,\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{k},X_{q_{k}})\right]\right|\leq\varepsilon,

(3.8)

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\chi(m_{i}^{(n-1)})\,\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})-\mathbb{E}\!\left[\chi(U)\,X_{q_{k}}\right]\right|\leq\varepsilon,

(3.9)

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\chi(m_{i}^{(n-1)})-\mathbb{E}\!\left[\chi(U)\right]\right|\leq\varepsilon

and, for every
k\in[n-1]
,

(3.10)

\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Phi_{\zeta}\!\Bigl(q_{k},\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})\Bigr)-\mathbb{E}\!\left[\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{k},X_{q_{k}})\right]\right|\leq\varepsilon.

Remark 3.1
.

The event
\mathrm{LawMatch}_{n-1}
enforces that, for each fixed
k\leq n-1
and for each test
function
\chi
in (
3.2
)–(
3.6
), the
\chi(m^{(n-1)}_{i})
–weighted
empirical averages of the coordinates match the corresponding moments under the law of

(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{1},X_{q_{1}}),\ldots,\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},X_{q_{n-1}}))
, up to error
\varepsilon

as in (
3.7
)–(
3.10
).

We now state the analogue of Proposition
2.2
.

Proposition 3.3

(Conditional annealed complexity)
.

Let
n\geq 2
. Let
\bm{u}=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n})
,

\bm{q}=(q_{1},\ldots,q_{n})
be strictly increasing
in
(0,1)
, and
\zeta\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{n+1}(\bm{u};\bm{q})
.
Define the
prefix free energy

(3.11)

\mathcal{P}^{(n)}(\bm{u};\bm{q})\;:=\;\inf_{\zeta^{\prime}\,\in\,\mathrm{Prefix}_{n+1}(\bm{u};\bm{q})}\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta^{\prime}),

and the
n
-step complexity functional at level
f

(3.12)

\mathcal{C}^{(n)}_{f}(\bm{u};\bm{q})\;:=\;u_{n}\,\bigl(\mathcal{P}^{(n)}(\bm{u};\bm{q})-f\bigr).

We assume:

(i)

(Tail optimality)
The infimum
\mathcal{P}^{(n)}(\bm{u};\bm{q})
is
attained at
\zeta
.

(ii)

(Stationarity)

(\bm{u};\bm{q})
is a critical point of

\mathcal{C}^{(n)}_{f}
.

Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, let

(\mathbf{m}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)})\in\mathrm{SUSY}_{n-1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)\cap\mathrm{LawMatch}_{n-1}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
. Let

\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q_{n},\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)})
be the number of critical points
\mathbf{m}

of
F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}
with

(\mathbf{m}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)},\mathbf{m})\in\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
and

\tfrac{1}{N}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})\in(f-\varepsilon,f+\varepsilon)
. Set

\mathrm{Skel}_{n-1}=\left(\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}^{(1)}),F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}^{(1)}),\ldots,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}),F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)})\right).

Then

(3.13)

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\mathcal{N}_{\varepsilon}(f,\zeta,q_{n},\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)})\;\Big|\;\mathrm{Skel}_{n-1}=(0,N\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta),\ldots,0,N\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta))\Bigr]=\mathcal{C}_{f}^{(n)}(\bm{u};\bm{q})\\
=\zeta([0,q_{n}))\,(\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta)-f).

Remark 3.2

(Consistency with the base case)
.

For
n=1
, one has
\bm{u}=(u)
,
\bm{q}=(q)
,
u_{n}=u=\zeta(\{0\})
, so
\mathcal{C}^{(n)}_{f}(\bm{u};\bm{q})=\mathcal{C}_{f}(u;q)
and
(
3.13
) recovers the conclusion of
Proposition
2.2
.

Remark 3.3

(On the choice of the free energy level of the ancestors)
.

Differentiating (
3.12
) with respect to
u_{l}=\zeta([0,q_{l}))
for
l\in[n-1]
forces the TAP free energy of the ancestors to equal
\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta)
(see (
3.29
)), while differentiating with respect to
u_{n}
gives the last-state condition (
3.30
), involving
f
. Moreover, taking
f=\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta)
in (
3.13
) gives a sub-extensive complexity (i.e., not growing exponentially).

At the thermodynamic equilibrium (
f=\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta)
), all TAP states have the same free energy, equal to the thermodynamic free energy, which is consistent with
[
18
, Theorem 4]
.

3.2.
Computing the conditional probability

In this subsection, we compute the joint Gaussian density of the gradients and values of
F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}
along the hierarchy
(\mathbf{m}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{m}^{(n)})
, extending Lemma
2.5
to
n
states.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. Let
\zeta
be a probability measure on
[0,1]
with prefix
\{0,q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\}
where
0<q_{1}<\ldots<q_{n}<1
. Let us emphasize that
\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\cap(q_{n},1]
can be nonempty. For every
j\in[n-1]
, set
\Delta_{j}:=-\zeta(\{q_{j}\})
and set
\Delta_{n}=\zeta([0,q_{n}))=\zeta([0,q_{n-1}])
. Let
f_{1},\ldots,f_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
. For every
\mathbf{m}\in[-1,1]^{N}
, denote
Z(\mathbf{m}):=(\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}),F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))
.

Let
(\mathbf{m}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{m}^{(n)})\in\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
where
\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
is as in Definition
3.1
. Moreover, assume that for every
k\in[n-1]
,

(3.14)

\sum_{i=1}^{N}(h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})m_{i}^{(k)})+\frac{1}{2}N\int_{0}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{2}N\int_{0}^{q_{k}}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\\
=-Nf_{k}+O(N\varepsilon^{1/2}).

For every
k\in[n]
, let
\mathrm{Skel}_{k}:=(Z(\mathbf{m}^{(1)}),\ldots,Z(\mathbf{m}^{(k)}))
.

Then,

(3.15)

\displaystyle\log\varphi_{\mathrm{Skel}_{n}\mid\mathrm{Skel}_{n-1}}(0,Nf_{1},\ldots,0,Nf_{n}\mid 0,Nf_{1},\ldots,0,Nf_{n-1})

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{2}N\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{n})\left(\int_{q_{n}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2(\xi^{\prime}(q_{n})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1}))}\left\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n},\mathbf{m}^{(n)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle-\Delta_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q_{n},m_{i}^{(n)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n},m_{i}^{(n)})m_{i}^{(n)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},m_{i}^{(n-1)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},m_{i}^{(n-1)})m_{i}^{(n-1)}\bigr)

\displaystyle-N\Delta_{n}(f_{n}\!-\!f_{n-1})-\tfrac{N}{2}\log\bigl(2\pi(\xi^{\prime}(q_{n})\!-\!\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1}))\bigr)+O(N\varepsilon^{1/2}).

Suppose in addition that

(3.16)

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q_{n},m_{i}^{(n)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n},m_{i}^{(n)})m_{i}^{(n)}\bigr)\\
+\tfrac{1}{2}N\!\int_{0}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t+\tfrac{1}{2}N\!\int_{0}^{q_{n}}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t=-Nf_{n}+O(N\varepsilon^{1/2}).

Then the inequality (
3.15
) is an equality.

We begin with a short auxiliary computation.

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
\zeta
be a probability measure on
[0,1]
with an
(n+1)
-atom prefix
\{0,q_{1},\ldots,q_{n}\}
, where
0<q_{1}<\cdots<q_{n}<1
.
Define
q_{ij}:=q_{\min(i,j)}
for
i,j\in[n]
, and set

\Delta_{k}:=\begin{cases}-\zeta(\{q_{k}\})&\text{if }k\in[n-1],\\
\zeta([0,q_{n}))&\text{if }k=n.\end{cases}

Let
(\mathbf{m}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{m}^{(n)})\in\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
, where
\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
is as in Definition
3.1
, and define

z^{(i)}:=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\xi^{\prime}(q_{ik})\,\Delta_{k}\,\mathbf{m}^{(k)},\qquad i\in[n].

Then for every
i,j\in[n]
,

\langle z^{(i)}+\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},\mathbf{m}^{(i)}),\,\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{ij})\int_{q_{j}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t+O(N\varepsilon).

Proof.

We verify the identity by treating the diagonal and off-diagonal cases separately.

Diagonal case:
i=j
.
Observe that
\sum_{k=i}^{n}\Delta_{k}=\zeta([0,q_{i}))
.
Expanding the inner product and using the overlap structure of
\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
yields

\langle z^{(i)},\mathbf{m}^{(i)}\rangle=N\sum_{k=1}^{i-1}\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})\,q_{k}\,\zeta(\{q_{k}\})-N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\,q_{i}\,\zeta\bigl([0,q_{i})\bigr)\\
=N\!\int_{0}^{q_{i}}\xi^{\prime}(t)\,t\,\mathrm{d}\zeta(t)-N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\,q_{i}\,\zeta\bigl([0,q_{i}]\bigr)+O(N\varepsilon).

Combining this with the definition of
\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
, we obtain

(3.17)

\langle z^{(i)}+\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},\mathbf{m}^{(i)}),\,\mathbf{m}^{(i)}\rangle=N\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\int_{q_{i}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t+O(N\varepsilon).

Off-diagonal case:
j\leq i
.
Since
q_{ik}=q_{k}
for
k\leq i
and
q_{jk}=q_{\min(j,k)}
, the inner product evaluates to

\langle z^{(i)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=-N\sum_{k=1}^{i}\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})\,\Delta_{k}\,q_{jk}-N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\,q_{j}\,\zeta\bigl([0,q_{i}]\bigr)\\
=N\int_{0}^{q_{i}}\xi^{\prime}(u)\,\min(u,q_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\zeta(u)-N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\,q_{j}\,\zeta\bigl([0,q_{i}]\bigr)+O(N\varepsilon).

Invoking the definition of
\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
then gives

\langle z^{(i)}+\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},\mathbf{m}^{(i)}),\,\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{j})\int_{q_{j}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t+O(N\varepsilon),

which is the desired identity since
q_{ij}=q_{j}
when
j\leq i
.

Off-diagonal case:
j\geq i
.
We instead compute the difference

\langle z^{(i)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}-\mathbf{m}^{(i)}\rangle=N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\biggl(\sum_{k=i+1}^{j}\zeta(\{q_{k}\})(q_{k}-q_{i})-(q_{j}-q_{i})\,\zeta\bigl([0,q_{j}]\bigr)\biggr)+O(N\varepsilon).

Applying Stieltjes integration by parts on
[q_{i},q_{j}]
,

\int_{(q_{i},q_{j}]}(t-q_{i})\,\mathrm{d}\zeta(t)+\int_{q_{i}}^{q_{j}}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t=(q_{j}-q_{i})\,\zeta\bigl([0,q_{j}]\bigr),

so the parenthetical expression above simplifies to

-\int_{q_{i}}^{q_{j}}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t
. Therefore

\langle z^{(i)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}-\mathbf{m}^{(i)}\rangle=-N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\int_{q_{i}}^{q_{j}}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t+O(N\varepsilon),

and by the definition of
\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
and (
B.15
),
the same relation holds with
z^{(i)}
replaced by

z^{(i)}+\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},\mathbf{m}^{(i)})
.
Adding this to the diagonal identity (
3.17
) yields

\langle z^{(i)}+\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},\mathbf{m}^{(i)}),\,\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\int_{q_{i}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t-N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\int_{q_{i}}^{q_{j}}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\\
=N\,\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\int_{q_{j}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t+O(N\varepsilon),

which is the claim since
q_{ij}=q_{i}
when
i\leq j
.
∎

We now turn to the proof of Lemma
3.4
.

Proof of Lemma
3.4
.

We prove the result assuming that all the constraints in
\mathrm{SUSY}_{n}(\zeta,\varepsilon)
hold exactly (i.e., with
\varepsilon=0
); the general case follows by a continuity argument analogous to the one in the proof of Lemma
2.5
, which introduces errors of order
O(N\varepsilon/\delta_{\varepsilon})=O(N\varepsilon^{1/2})
(since
\delta_{\varepsilon}=\varepsilon^{1/2}
).

Set
q_{0}=0
and, for
i,j\in[n]
, set
q_{ij}:=q_{\min(i,j)}
.

Step 1: the convex dual identity

By Lemma
B.8
, (
B.35
), for every
k\in[n]
,

\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}^{(k)})=\nabla H(\mathbf{m}^{(k)})-k_{\zeta}(q_{k},\mathbf{m}^{(k)})+O(\varepsilon),

where
k_{\zeta}
is as in (
1.22
) and the error term is understood componentwise. Moreover, recall that

F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}^{(k)})=H(\mathbf{m}^{(k)})-\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})-N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{k}),

where
h_{\zeta}
is as in (
1.21
) and
\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}
as in (
1.20
). Set

\Gamma_{n}:=\mathrm{Cov}\bigl[\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}^{(1)}),\ldots,\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}^{(n)})\bigr],\qquad\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}:=\mathrm{Cov}[\mathrm{Skel}_{n}].

Let
a_{n}=(a_{n}^{(1)},\ldots,a_{n}^{(n)})
where, for every
k\in[n]
,

a_{n}^{(k)}:=\Bigl(k_{\zeta}(q_{k},\mathbf{m}^{(k)}),\;Nf_{k}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})+N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{k})\Bigr)\in\mathbb{R}^{N+1}.

By the above,
\mathrm{Skel}_{n}=(Z(\mathbf{m}^{(1)}),\ldots,Z(\mathbf{m}^{(n)}))
is a Gaussian vector of covariance matrix
\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}
(positive definite since
\xi
is a mixed model; see the proof of Lemma
2.5
); hence

(3.18)

\log\varphi_{\mathrm{Skel}_{n}}(0,Nf_{1},\ldots,0,Nf_{n})=-\tfrac{1}{2}\langle a_{n},(\Gamma_{n}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n}\rangle-\tfrac{1}{2}\log\det(2\pi\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}).

Using the convex dual identity

-\langle a_{n},(\Gamma_{n}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n}\rangle=\inf_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{n(N+1)}}\bigl\{\langle w,\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}w\rangle-2\langle w,a_{n}\rangle\bigr\},

we write
w=(x^{(1)},v_{1},\ldots,x^{(n)},v_{n})
with
x^{(k)}\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
v_{k}\in\mathbb{R}
, so that

(3.19)

-\langle a_{n},(\Gamma_{n}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n}\rangle=\inf_{\begin{subarray}{c}x^{(k)}\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\,v_{k}\in\mathbb{R}\\
k\in[n]\end{subarray}}\bigl(\langle w,\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}w\rangle-2\langle w,a_{n}\rangle\bigr).

Observe next that the covariance identities for
H
, together
with the hierarchical orthogonality

\langle\mathbf{m}^{(j)},\mathbf{m}^{(i+1)}-\mathbf{m}^{(i)}\rangle=0\quad\text{for }j\leq i\qquad(\text{so that }\langle\mathbf{m}^{(i)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=Nq_{ij}),

yield

\langle w,\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}w\rangle=\langle x,\Gamma_{n}x\rangle+N\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\xi(q_{ij})v_{i}v_{j}+2\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\xi^{\prime}(q_{ij})v_{j}\langle x^{(i)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle.

Hence, taking
v_{i}:=\Delta_{i}
for every
i\in[n]
in (
3.19
) yields

(3.20)

\displaystyle-\langle a_{n},(\Gamma_{n}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n}\rangle\leq

\displaystyle N\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\xi(q_{ij})\Delta_{i}\Delta_{j}-2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},m_{k}^{(i)})+N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{i})+Nf_{i}\Bigr)

\displaystyle-N\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{i})\Bigl(\int_{q_{i}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\Bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle+\inf_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{n}}\Bigl(\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\xi^{\prime}(q_{ij})\langle x^{(i)},x^{(j)}\rangle-2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigl\langle\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},\mathbf{m}^{(i)})-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi^{\prime}(q_{ij})\Delta_{j}\mathbf{m}^{(j)},\,x^{(i)}\Bigr\rangle\Bigr),

where

\mathcal{D}_{n}:=\Bigl\{x\in(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{n}:\ \forall\,i,j\in[n],\ \langle x^{(i)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=N\mathbf{1}_{\{i=j\}}\int_{q_{i}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\Bigr\}.

Define, for
i\in[n]
,

(3.21)

y_{n}^{(i)}:=\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},\mathbf{m}^{(i)})-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi^{\prime}(q_{ij})\Delta_{j}\mathbf{m}^{(j)},\qquad y_{n}^{(0)}:=0.

Then the infimum in (
3.20
) can be rewritten as

(3.22)

\inf_{x\in\mathcal{D}_{n}}\bigl(\langle x,(\xi^{\prime}(Q)\otimes I_{N})x\rangle-2\langle x,y_{n}\rangle\bigr),\qquad Q=(q_{ij})_{i,j\in[n]}.

Notice that, since
\sum_{k=i}^{n}\Delta_{k}=\zeta([0,q_{i}))
,

\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\xi(q_{ij})\Delta_{i}\Delta_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi(q_{i})-\xi(q_{i-1}))\Bigl(\sum_{k=i}^{n}\Delta_{k}\Bigr)^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi(q_{i})-\xi(q_{i-1}))\zeta([0,q_{i}))^{2}.

Step 2: optimization over
x

Introduce the change of variables
z^{(k)}:=\sum_{i=k}^{n}x^{(i)}
. Then

\langle x,(\xi^{\prime}(Q)\otimes I_{N})x\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}(\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})\!-\!\xi^{\prime}(q_{k-1}))\|z^{(k)}\|^{2},\qquad\langle x,y_{n}\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\langle z^{(k)},y_{n}^{(k)}-y_{n}^{(k-1)}\rangle.

Hence, the unconstrained infimum satisfies

\inf_{x\in(\mathbb{R}^{N})^{n}}\bigl(\langle x,(\xi^{\prime}(Q)\otimes I_{N})x\rangle-2\langle x,y_{n}\rangle\bigr)=-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{\|y_{n}^{(k)}-y_{n}^{(k-1)}\|^{2}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{k-1})},

and the unique minimizer is given by

(3.23)

z_{n}^{(k)}:=\frac{y_{n}^{(k)}-y_{n}^{(k-1)}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{k-1})},\qquad k\in[n].

Let
L\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be the lower-triangular matrix
L_{ik}:=\mathbf{1}_{\{k\leq i\}}
, so that
z^{(k)}=\sum_{i=k}^{n}x^{(i)}=(L^{T}x)^{(k)}
.
Let
x_{n}:=((L^{-1})^{T}\otimes I_{N})z_{n}
, i.e.
x_{n}^{(k)}=z_{n}^{(k)}-z_{n}^{(k+1)}
with
z_{n}^{(n+1)}:=0
.
We claim that
x_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{n}
, so the restriction to
\mathcal{D}_{n}
in (
3.22
) is not lossy.

By Lemma
3.5
, for every
i,j\in[n]
,

(3.24)

\langle y_{n}^{(i)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=N\xi^{\prime}(q_{ij})\int_{q_{j}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t.

Fix
k\in[n]
.
If
j<k
, then
q_{kj}=q_{k-1,j}=q_{j}
, so (
3.24
) gives

\langle y_{n}^{(k)}-y_{n}^{(k-1)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=0
.
If
j\geq k
, then
q_{kj}=q_{k}
and
q_{k-1,j}=q_{k-1}
, so

\langle y_{n}^{(k)}-y_{n}^{(k-1)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=N(\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{k-1}))\int_{q_{j}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t
.
Therefore, by (
3.23
),

\langle z_{n}^{(k)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=\begin{cases}0,&j<k,\\[2.0pt]
N\displaystyle\int_{q_{j}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t,&j\geq k.\end{cases}

Since
x_{n}^{(k)}=z_{n}^{(k)}-z_{n}^{(k+1)}
, it follows that for every
j\in[n]
,

\langle x_{n}^{(k)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=\begin{cases}0,&j\neq k,\\[2.0pt]
N\displaystyle\int_{q_{k}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t,&j=k,\end{cases}

i.e.
x_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{n}
, as claimed.

Inserting the value of the infimum into (
3.20
) then yields

(3.25)

\displaystyle-\langle a_{n},(\Gamma_{n}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n}\rangle\leq

\displaystyle N\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi(q_{i})-\xi(q_{i-1}))\zeta([0,q_{i}))^{2}-2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},m_{k}^{(i)})+N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{i})+Nf_{i}\Bigr)

\displaystyle-N\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{i})\Bigl(\int_{q_{i}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\Bigr)^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\|y_{n}^{(i)}-y_{n}^{(i-1)}\|^{2}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1})}.

Step 3: equality in (
3.25
) under (
3.16
)

Suppose that (
3.16
) holds. In view of the computations in Steps 1–2, the inequality (
3.25
) is an equality if and only if
w=(x_{n}^{(1)},\Delta_{1},\ldots,x_{n}^{(n)},\Delta_{n})
is the minimizer of
w^{\prime}\mapsto\frac{1}{2}\langle w^{\prime},\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}w^{\prime}\rangle-\langle w^{\prime},a_{n}\rangle
. Since the objective in (
3.19
) is strictly convex, this holds if and only if
\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}w=a_{n}
, which is equivalent to

(3.26)

\begin{cases}\langle x_{n}^{(i)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle=N\mathbf{1}_{\{i=j\}}\displaystyle\int_{q_{i}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t,\qquad&i,j\in[n],\\[6.0pt]
\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})\langle x_{n}^{(k)},\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\rangle+N\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi(q_{kj})\Delta_{j}-\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})-N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{k})=Nf_{k},\qquad&k\in[n].\end{cases}

The first condition is precisely
x_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{n}
, which was established in Step 2. It remains to verify the second line. Fix
k\in[n]
and write

\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})m_{i}^{(k)}\bigr)+\langle\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},\mathbf{m}^{(k)}),\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\rangle.

By
x_{n}\in\mathcal{D}_{n}
we have
\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})\langle x_{n}^{(k)},\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\rangle=N\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})\int_{q_{k}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t
. Moreover, taking
i=j=k
in (
3.24
) and subtracting

\bigl\langle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi^{\prime}(q_{kj})\Delta_{j}\mathbf{m}^{(j)},\,\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\bigr\rangle
from both sides of (
3.24
) gives

\langle\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},\mathbf{m}^{(k)}),\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\rangle=N\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})\int_{q_{k}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t-N\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\xi^{\prime}(q_{j})q_{j}\zeta(\{q_{j}\})+N\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})q_{k}\zeta([0,q_{k})).

Observe next that

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi(q_{kj})\Delta_{j}=-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\xi(q_{j})\zeta(\{q_{j}\})+\xi(q_{k})\sum_{j=k}^{n}\Delta_{j}=-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\xi(q_{j})\zeta(\{q_{j}\})+\xi(q_{k})\zeta([0,q_{k})).

Combining the last three displays yields

\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})\langle x_{n}^{(k)},\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\rangle+N\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi(q_{kj})\Delta_{j}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})-N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{k})\\
\qquad=-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})m_{i}^{(k)}\bigr)-N\zeta([0,q_{k}))\!\int_{0}^{q_{k}}\!t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t+N\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\zeta(\{q_{j}\})\!\int_{0}^{q_{j}}\!t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t\\
-N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{k}).

Using Stieltjes integration by parts,

\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\zeta(\{q_{j}\})\int_{0}^{q_{j}}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t=\zeta([0,q_{k}))\int_{0}^{q_{k}}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t-\int_{0}^{q_{k}}\zeta([0,t])\,t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t,

we obtain

\xi^{\prime}(q_{k})\langle x_{n}^{(k)},\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\rangle+N\sum_{j=1}^{n}\xi(q_{kj})\Delta_{j}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})-N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{k})\\
=-\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{k},m_{i}^{(k)})m_{i}^{(k)}\bigr)-\frac{N}{2}\!\int_{0}^{1}\!t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t-\frac{N}{2}\!\int_{0}^{q_{k}}\!t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t.

For
k\leq n-1
this equals
Nf_{k}
by (
3.14
), and for
k=n
it equals
Nf_{n}
by (
3.16
). Thus the second condition in (
3.26
) holds, and therefore
\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}w=a_{n}
, so that (
3.25
) is an equality:

(3.27)

\displaystyle-\langle a_{n},(\Gamma_{n}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n}\rangle=

\displaystyle N\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi(q_{i})-\xi(q_{i-1}))\zeta([0,q_{i}))^{2}-2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Delta_{i}\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},m_{k}^{(i)})+N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{i})+Nf_{i}\Bigr)

\displaystyle-N\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{i})\Bigl(\int_{q_{i}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\Bigr)^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\|y_{n}^{(i)}-y_{n}^{(i-1)}\|^{2}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1})}.

Step 4: conclusion

Let
a_{n-1}
,
\Gamma_{n-1}^{\prime}
be defined as above with
\mathrm{Skel}_{n-1}
in place of
\mathrm{Skel}_{n}
.
Since the conditional density of a Gaussian vector is Gaussian,

\log\varphi_{\mathrm{Skel}_{n}\mid\mathrm{Skel}_{n-1}}(0,Nf_{1},\ldots,0,Nf_{n}\mid 0,Nf_{1},\ldots,0,Nf_{n-1})\\
=\log\varphi_{\mathrm{Skel}_{n}}(0,Nf_{1},\ldots,0,Nf_{n})-\log\varphi_{\mathrm{Skel}_{n-1}}(0,Nf_{1},\ldots,0,Nf_{n-1}).

In view of (
3.18
), it suffices to compare the quadratic forms and the determinants at levels
n
and
n-1
.

We begin with the quadratic forms. For the
(n\!-\!1)
-level computation, the relevant weights are

\tilde{\Delta}_{j}:=-\zeta(\{q_{j}\})\quad(1\leq j\leq n-2),\qquad\tilde{\Delta}_{n-1}:=\zeta([0,q_{n-1}))=\Delta_{n-1}+\Delta_{n},

and we define
y_{n-1}^{(i)}
by (
3.21
) with
n
replaced by
n-1
and
\Delta
replaced by
\tilde{\Delta}
.
Since (
3.14
) holds up to
k=n-1
, Step 3 applied at level
n-1
yields the equality

(3.28)

\displaystyle-\langle a_{n-1},(\Gamma_{n-1}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n-1}\rangle=

\displaystyle N\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(\xi(q_{i})-\xi(q_{i-1}))\zeta([0,q_{i}))^{2}-2\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\tilde{\Delta}_{i}\Bigl(\sum_{k=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{i},m_{k}^{(i)})+N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{i})+Nf_{i}\Bigr)

\displaystyle-N\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{i})\Bigl(\int_{q_{i}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\Bigr)^{\!2}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\frac{\|y_{n-1}^{(i)}\!-\!y_{n-1}^{(i-1)}\|^{2}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\!-\!\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1})}.

Combining the inequality (
3.25
) at level
n
with the equality (
3.28
) at level
n-1
, we get

-\frac{1}{2}\langle a_{n},(\Gamma_{n}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle a_{n-1},(\Gamma_{n-1}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n-1}\rangle\leq\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\mathrm{RHS}_{n}-\mathrm{RHS}_{n-1}\bigr),

where
\mathrm{RHS}_{n}
and
\mathrm{RHS}_{n-1}
denote the right-hand sides of (
3.25
) and (
3.28
) respectively.
All terms with index
\leq n-2
cancel. Using
\tilde{\Delta}_{n-1}=\Delta_{n-1}+\Delta_{n}
, we obtain

\mathrm{RHS}_{n}-\mathrm{RHS}_{n-1}=N(\xi(q_{n})-\xi(q_{n-1}))\zeta([0,q_{n}))^{2}-N\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{n})\Bigl(\int_{q_{n}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\Bigr)^{2}\\
-2\Delta_{n}\Bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{n},m_{i}^{(n)})+N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{n})+Nf_{n}-\sum_{i=1}^{N}h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},m_{i}^{(n-1)})-N\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{n-1})-Nf_{n-1}\Bigr)\\
-\Biggl[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\|y_{n}^{(i)}-y_{n}^{(i-1)}\|^{2}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\!-\!\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1})}-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\frac{\|y_{n-1}^{(i)}-y_{n-1}^{(i-1)}\|^{2}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\!-\!\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1})}\Biggr].

We now compute the difference of the squared-norm sums. From the definitions of
y_{n}
and
y_{n-1}
and the identity
\tilde{\Delta}_{n-1}=\Delta_{n-1}+\Delta_{n}
, for every
i\leq n-1
,

y_{n}^{(i)}=y_{n-1}^{(i)}-\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\Delta_{n}\,(\mathbf{m}^{(n)}-\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}),\qquad\text{hence}\\
y_{n}^{(i)}-y_{n}^{(i-1)}=\bigl(y_{n-1}^{(i)}-y_{n-1}^{(i-1)}\bigr)-(\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\!-\!\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1}))\Delta_{n}\,(\mathbf{m}^{(n)}-\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}).

Using
\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(y_{n-1}^{(i)}-y_{n-1}^{(i-1)})=y_{n-1}^{(n-1)}
,
the telescoping identity
\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1}))=\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1})
,
and
\|\mathbf{m}^{(n)}-\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}\|^{2}=N(q_{n}-q_{n-1})
, we compute

\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\frac{\|y_{n}^{(i)}-y_{n}^{(i-1)}\|^{2}-\|y_{n-1}^{(i)}-y_{n-1}^{(i-1)}\|^{2}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1})}\\
=-2\Delta_{n}\langle y_{n-1}^{(n-1)},\mathbf{m}^{(n)}-\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}\rangle+N\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1})\Delta_{n}^{2}(q_{n}-q_{n-1}).

Moreover, by definition,

y_{n}^{(n)}-y_{n}^{(n-1)}=\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n},\mathbf{m}^{(n)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)})-(\xi^{\prime}(q_{n})\!-\!\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1}))\Delta_{n}\,\mathbf{m}^{(n)}.

Since
\mathbf{m}^{(n)}-\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}\perp\mathrm{Span}\{\mathbf{m}^{(1)},\ldots,\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}\}
, the definition (
3.21
) at level
n-1
gives

\langle y_{n-1}^{(n-1)},\mathbf{m}^{(n)}-\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}\rangle=\langle\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},\allowbreak\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}),\mathbf{m}^{(n)}-\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}\rangle
.
Expanding the squares and using

\lVert\mathbf{m}^{(n)}\rVert^{2}=Nq_{n}
, we obtain

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\|y_{n}^{(i)}-y_{n}^{(i-1)}\|^{2}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\!-\!\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1})}-\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\frac{\|y_{n-1}^{(i)}-y_{n-1}^{(i-1)}\|^{2}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})\!-\!\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1})}=\frac{\bigl\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n},\mathbf{m}^{(n)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)})\bigr\|^{2}}{\xi^{\prime}(q_{n})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1})}\\
-2\Delta_{n}\Bigl(\langle\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n},\mathbf{m}^{(n)}),\mathbf{m}^{(n)}\rangle-\langle\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}),\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)}\rangle\Bigr)+N\Delta_{n}^{2}\bigl(\xi^{\prime}(q_{n})q_{n}-\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1})q_{n-1}\bigr).

Plugging this into
\mathrm{RHS}_{n}-\mathrm{RHS}_{n-1}
and grouping terms yields

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\langle a_{n},(\Gamma_{n}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n}\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle a_{n-1},(\Gamma_{n-1}^{\prime})^{-1}a_{n-1}\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\leq-\frac{N}{2}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{n})\Bigl(\int_{q_{n}}^{1}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t\Bigr)^{2}-\frac{\bigl\|\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n},\mathbf{m}^{(n)})-\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},\mathbf{m}^{(n-1)})\bigr\|^{2}}{2(\xi^{\prime}(q_{n})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1}))}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\Delta_{n}\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q_{n},m_{i}^{(n)})\!-\!\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n},m_{i}^{(n)})m_{i}^{(n)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\Delta_{n}\!\sum_{i=1}^{N}\bigl(h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},m_{i}^{(n-1)})\!-\!\partial_{m}h_{\zeta}(q_{n-1},m_{i}^{(n-1)})m_{i}^{(n-1)}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-N\Delta_{n}(f_{n}-f_{n-1})-N\Delta_{n}\bigl(\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{n})-\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{n-1})\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\tfrac{N\Delta_{n}^{2}}{2}\bigl(\xi(q_{n})\!-\!q_{n}\xi^{\prime}(q_{n})

\displaystyle\hskip 80.00012pt-\xi(q_{n-1})\!+\!q_{n-1}\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1})\bigr).

Since
\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\cap(q_{n-1},q_{n})=\varnothing
, we have
\zeta([0,t])=\Delta_{n}
for all
t\in[q_{n-1},q_{n})
. Hence

\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{n})-\mathcal{U}_{\zeta}(q_{n-1})=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{q_{n-1}}^{q_{n}}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t=-\frac{\Delta_{n}}{2}\int_{q_{n-1}}^{q_{n}}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

Moreover, using
(\xi(q)-q\xi^{\prime}(q))^{\prime}=-q\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)
,

\xi(q_{n})-q_{n}\xi^{\prime}(q_{n})-\xi(q_{n-1})+q_{n-1}\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1})=-\int_{q_{n-1}}^{q_{n}}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t.

These two identities show that the last two terms cancel, yielding exactly the right-hand side of (
3.15
) without the determinant contribution.

We now turn to the determinant. Write the covariance of
\mathrm{Skel}_{n}
in block form

\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}G_{n}&C_{n}\\
C_{n}^{T}&E_{n}\end{pmatrix},

where
G_{n}
is the covariance of the
n
gradients (dimension
nN\times nN
) and
E_{n}
is the covariance of the
n
energies (dimension
n\times n
).

Recall that
\mathrm{Cov}(H(\mathbf{m}),H(\mathbf{m}^{\prime}))=N\xi(\langle\mathbf{m},\mathbf{m}^{\prime}\rangle/N)
, hence for
i,j\in[n]
and coordinates
a,b\in[N]
,

\mathrm{Cov}(\partial_{a}H(\mathbf{m}^{(i)}),\partial_{b}H(\mathbf{m}^{(j)}))=\xi^{\prime}(q_{ij})\mathbf{1}_{\{a=b\}}+\frac{1}{N}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{ij})\,m^{(j)}_{a}\,m^{(i)}_{b},\qquad q_{ij}=\frac{1}{N}\langle\mathbf{m}^{(i)},\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\rangle.

Equivalently, the
(i,j)
block of
G_{n}
is

(G_{n})_{ij}=\xi^{\prime}(q_{ij})I_{N}+\frac{1}{N}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{ij})\,\mathbf{m}^{(j)}(\mathbf{m}^{(i)})^{T}.

Therefore,

G_{n}=(\xi^{\prime}(Q)\otimes I_{N})+\frac{1}{N}R_{n},\qquad R_{n}:=\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{ij})\,(e_{i}\otimes\mathbf{m}^{(j)})(e_{j}\otimes\mathbf{m}^{(i)})^{T},

so
R_{n}
has rank at most
n^{2}
(hence
G_{n}
is a finite-rank perturbation of
\xi^{\prime}(Q)\otimes I_{N}
).

Let
A:=\xi^{\prime}(Q)\otimes I_{N}
. Since
\xi^{\prime}(Q)\succ 0
,
A
is invertible and

\det G_{n}=\det A\cdot\det\!\bigl(I_{nN}+A^{-1}(N^{-1}R_{n})\bigr).

Now
A^{-1}=(\xi^{\prime}(Q)^{-1}\otimes I_{N})
and
\|\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\|^{2}=\langle\mathbf{m}^{(k)},\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\rangle=Nq_{k}
, so each rank-one term

\frac{1}{N}\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{ij})(e_{i}\otimes\mathbf{m}^{(j)})(e_{j}\otimes\mathbf{m}^{(i)})^{T}

has operator norm
O(1)
, and
\mathrm{rank}(R_{n})\leq n^{2}
. Hence
A^{-1}(N^{-1}R_{n})
has rank
\leq n^{2}
and bounded operator norm, so

\log\det G_{n}=\log\det A+O(1)=N\log\det\xi^{\prime}(Q)+O(1),

since
\det(\xi^{\prime}(Q)\otimes I_{N})=\det(\xi^{\prime}(Q))^{N}
.

Applying the Schur complement formula,

\det\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}=\det G_{n}\cdot\det\bigl(E_{n}-C_{n}^{T}G_{n}^{-1}C_{n}\bigr),

where the second determinant is over an
n\times n
matrix. Its entries are
O(N)
(indeed
E_{n}
has entries
N\xi(q_{ij})
and

C_{n}
has entries of order
\|\mathbf{m}^{(j)}\|=O(\sqrt{N})
), so it contributes
O(\log N)
to
\log\det\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}
.
Consequently,

\log\det\Gamma_{n}^{\prime}=N\log\det\xi^{\prime}(Q)+O(\log N),\qquad\log\det\Gamma_{n-1}^{\prime}=N\log\det\xi^{\prime}(Q_{n-1})+O(\log N).

Since
\xi^{\prime}(Q)
has the
LDL^{T}
factorization with
L_{ik}=\mathbf{1}_{\{k\leq i\}}
, we have

\det\xi^{\prime}(Q)=\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1}))
and
\det\xi^{\prime}(Q_{n-1})=\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}(\xi^{\prime}(q_{i})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{i-1}))
.
Therefore,

-\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{\det(2\pi\Gamma_{n}^{\prime})}{\det(2\pi\Gamma_{n-1}^{\prime})}=-\frac{N}{2}\log\bigl(2\pi(\xi^{\prime}(q_{n})-\xi^{\prime}(q_{n-1}))\bigr)+O(\log N),

and combining with the quadratic-form bound yields (
3.15
).

Finally, if in addition (
3.16
) holds, then (
3.25
) is an equality by Step 3; since (
3.28
) is already an equality, the bound (
3.15
) holds with equality. This concludes the proof.
∎

3.3.
Deriving optimality conditions

In this subsection, we derive necessary conditions satisfied by the optimizer
\zeta
in the assumption of Proposition
3.3
, extending the optimality consequences of Lemma
2.9
to the hierarchical setting.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
n\geq 2
and let
(u_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}
and
(q_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n}
be strictly increasing sequences of numbers in
(0,1)
. Let
\zeta\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{n+1}((u_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n},(q_{i})_{1\leq i\leq n})
where
\mathrm{Prefix}_{n+1}
is as in Definition
1.2
. Suppose that
\zeta
satisfies the assumptions of Proposition
3.3
. Let
(X_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
be the Auffinger–Chen process

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}X_{t}=\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(t,X_{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)}\mathrm{d}B_{t}\\
X_{0}=0.\end{cases}

For every
s\in[0,1]
, set

H_{\zeta}(s):=\frac{1}{2}\int_{s}^{1}\xi^{\prime\prime}(r)\Bigl(\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(r,X_{r}))^{2}]-r\Bigr)\mathrm{d}r.

Then, the following hold:

(1)

For every
k\in[n-1]
,

(3.29)

-\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{k},X_{q_{k}})]+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{q_{k}}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t=-\mathcal{P}\mathrm{arisi}(\zeta),

(2)

(3.30)

-\mathbb{E}[\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{n},X_{q_{n}})]+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{q_{n}}t\xi^{\prime\prime}(t)\zeta([0,t])\mathrm{d}t=-f,

(3)

For every
k\in[n]
,

(3.31)

\mathbb{E}[(\partial_{x}\Phi_{\zeta}(q_{k},X_{q_{k}}))^{2}]=q_{k},

(4)

(3.32)

\mathrm{supp}(\zeta)\cap[q_{n},1]\subset\underset{[q_{n},1]}{\mathrm{arg\,min}}(H_{\zeta}).

Proof.

The proof follows the same strategy as the proof of Lemma
2.9
; we only sketch the differences.

Write
\zeta=u_{1}\delta_{0}+(1-u_{1})\lambda
with
\lambda\in\mathcal{P}([q_{1},1])
and
q_{1}\in\mathrm{supp}(\lambda)
. Set
\lambda^{\prime}:=(1-\theta)\lambda+\theta\lambda_{0}
for a perturbation
\lambda_{0}\in\mathcal{P}([q_{1},1])
with
q_{1}\in\mathrm{supp}(\lambda_{0})
, and define
\zeta_{\theta}:=u_{1}\delta_{0}+(1-u_{1})\lambda^{\prime}
. Then
\zeta_{\theta}\in\mathrm{Prefix}_{n+1}(\bm{u};\bm{q})
