\int\partial_{xx}\Phi_{\zeta}(q,x)\,\mathrm{d}\nu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}(x)=\int_{q}^{1}\zeta([0,s])\,\mathrm{d}s-\Delta_{\zeta}^{\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}.

Inserting this into (
B.39
) yields
(
B.34
).

If
\zeta=\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}
, then by Lemma
B.6
(iv)
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[u_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(t,X_{t}^{\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})},\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}})^{2}\bigr]=t\qquad\text{for every }t\in\mathrm{supp}(\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}),

hence
\Delta_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}^{\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}=0
. Therefore
(
B.34
) gives

-N\partial_{i}\mathrm{TAP}(\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})},\zeta_{\mathbf{m}})=k_{\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}}(q,m_{i}).

Since
\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}
is the minimizer in the definition of
\mathrm{TAP}(\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})})
,
the envelope theorem implies

\partial_{i}\mathrm{TAP}(\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})})=\partial_{i}\mathrm{TAP}(\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})},\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}),

which proves (
B.33
).

Finally, assume that
\max_{j\in[N]}|m_{j}|\leq 1-\delta
and

\zeta(\{q\})\geq u_{0}>0
. Then
\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}
is supported in

[-1+\delta,1-\delta]
, so Lemma
B.7
gives

|\Delta_{\zeta}^{\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}|\leq\frac{C(\xi,u_{0})}{\delta}\,\mathrm{dist}(\zeta,\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}).

Using (
B.34
) and
|m_{i}|\leq 1
, we obtain

\Bigl|\partial_{i}\mathrm{TAP}(\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})},\zeta)+\frac{1}{N}k_{\zeta}(q,m_{i})\Bigr|=\frac{|m_{i}|\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}\,|\Delta_{\zeta}^{\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}|\leq\frac{C}{N\delta}\,\mathrm{dist}(\zeta,\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}),

which proves (
B.35
).
∎

B.4.
Stability of the TAP correction

In this subsection, we show that the minimizer of
\mathrm{TAP}(\mu,\cdot)
depends continuously on the empirical measure
\mu
and on the overlap
q
. This stability is used in the main proofs to pass from finite-
N
empirical measures to their deterministic limits.

Recall the metric
\mathrm{dist}
on
\mathcal{P}([0,1])
(
1.23
).

Lemma B.9

(Stability of the TAP minimizer)
.

Fix
q\in(0,1)
and let
\mu\in\mathcal{P}([-1,1])
with
\int m^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}m)=q
. Let
\zeta_{\star}\in\mathcal{P}([q,1])
be the unique minimizer of
\mathrm{TAP}(\mu,\cdot)

and for every
N
, let
\zeta_{N}
be the unique minimizer of
\mathrm{TAP}(\mu_{N},\cdot)
,
where
q_{N}:=\int m^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mu_{N}(m)
.
If
q_{N}\to q
and
\mu_{N}\Rightarrow\mu
weakly, then

\mathrm{dist}(\zeta_{N},\zeta_{\star})\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0.

In particular, if
\int m^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}m)=q
,

m_{1},\ldots,m_{N}
are i.i.d. with law
\mu
,

q_{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}m_{i}^{2}
,

\mu_{N}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{m_{i}}
, and
\zeta_{\mathbf{m}}
denotes the minimizer in
Definition
1.1
, then for every
\varepsilon>0
,

\mu^{\otimes N}\!\left(\mathrm{dist}(\zeta_{\mathbf{m}},\zeta_{\star})>\varepsilon\right)\xrightarrow[N\to\infty]{}0.

Proof.

This follows from
[
18
, Proof of Lemma 22]
.
∎

Appendix C
Random matrices computations

C.1.
Conditional law of the Hessian

In this subsection, we decompose the Hessian
\nabla^{2}H(\mathbf{m})
into blocks along
\mathbf{m}
and
\mathbf{m}^{\perp}
, and compute the conditional law of each block given the value and gradient of
H
at
\mathbf{m}
.

Lemma C.1
.

Fix
\mathbf{m}\in[-1,1]^{N}
and set
q:=\|\mathbf{m}\|^{2}/N\in[0,1]
.
Assume
q>0
(the case
q=0
does not arise in our applications).
Choose an orthonormal basis

e_{1}=\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\sqrt{Nq}},\qquad e_{2},\dots,e_{N}\in\mathbf{m}^{\perp},

and decompose the Hessian
\nabla^{2}H(\mathbf{m})
in this basis as

\nabla^{2}H(\mathbf{m})=\begin{pmatrix}A&B^{\!\top}\\[2.84526pt]
B&C\end{pmatrix},\quad A\in\mathbb{R},\quad B\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1},\quad C\in\mathbb{R}^{(N-1)\times(N-1)}.

Condition on
H(\mathbf{m})=Nf
and
\nabla H(\mathbf{m})=x
.
Write
x_{\parallel}:=\langle x,e_{1}\rangle
and let

x_{\perp}:=\bigl(\langle x,e_{2}\rangle,\dots,\langle x,e_{N}\rangle\bigr)\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}

be the coordinate vector of the projection of
x
onto
\mathbf{m}^{\perp}
in the basis
(e_{2},\dots,e_{N})
.
The following assertions hold.

(i)

(Tangent block.)

The block
C
is independent of
\bigl(H(\mathbf{m}),\nabla H(\mathbf{m})\bigr)
, and its entries satisfy

\mathrm{Cov}(C_{ij},C_{kl})=\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}\bigl(\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\bigr),\qquad i,j,k,l\in\{2,\dots,N\}.

Equivalently (under the convention that
\mathrm{GOE}_{n}
has off-diagonal variance
1
and diagonal variance
2
),

C\ \stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\ \sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}\;\frac{\mathrm{GOE}_{N-1}}{\sqrt{N}}.

(ii)

(Mixed block.)

Conditionally on
\bigl(H(\mathbf{m}),\nabla H(\mathbf{m})\bigr)
, the vector
B
is Gaussian and independent of
C
, with

(C.1)

\mathbb{E}\!\left[B\;\middle|\;H(\mathbf{m})=Nf,\;\nabla H(\mathbf{m})=x\right]=\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{\xi^{\prime}(q)}\,\sqrt{\frac{q}{N}}\;x_{\perp},

(C.2)

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\;\middle|\;H(\mathbf{m})=Nf,\;\nabla H(\mathbf{m})=x\right)=\frac{1}{N}\!\left(\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)+q\,\xi^{(3)}(q)-\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)^{2}\,q}{\xi^{\prime}(q)}\right)I_{N-1}.

(iii)

(Longitudinal entry.)

Assume that
\det\Sigma_{X}>0
(this holds whenever
\xi
is a mixed model).
Define

(C.3)

\Sigma_{X}:=\begin{pmatrix}N\xi(q)&\xi^{\prime}(q)\sqrt{Nq}\\[2.84526pt]
\xi^{\prime}(q)\sqrt{Nq}&\xi^{\prime}(q)+q\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\end{pmatrix},

(C.4)

\Sigma_{A,X}:=\Bigl(\;\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\,q\;,\;\bigl(\xi^{(3)}(q)\,q+2\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\bigr)\sqrt{q/N}\;\Bigr),

(C.5)

\mathrm{Var}(A)=\frac{2\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}+\frac{4\,\xi^{(3)}(q)\,q}{N}+\frac{\xi^{(4)}(q)\,q^{2}}{N}.

Then, conditionally on
\bigl(H(\mathbf{m}),\nabla H(\mathbf{m})\bigr)
, the entry
A
is Gaussian and independent of
C
, with

(C.6)

\mathbb{E}\!\left[A\;\middle|\;H(\mathbf{m})=Nf,\;\nabla H(\mathbf{m})=x\right]=\;\Sigma_{A,X}\;\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\;\binom{Nf}{x_{\parallel}},

(C.7)

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(A\;\middle|\;H(\mathbf{m})=Nf,\;\nabla H(\mathbf{m})=x\right)=\;\mathrm{Var}(A)\;-\;\Sigma_{A,X}\;\Sigma_{X}^{-1}\;\Sigma_{A,X}^{\!\top}.

(iv)

(Joint structure.)

Conditionally on
\bigl(H(\mathbf{m})=Nf,\nabla H(\mathbf{m})=x\bigr)
, the triple
(A,B,C)
is jointly Gaussian.
Moreover,
C
is independent of
(A,B)
, and
A
and
B
are conditionally independent as well (so
(A,B,C)
are conditionally independent given
(H(\mathbf{m}),\nabla H(\mathbf{m}))
).

Proof.

Throughout, we work in the orthonormal basis
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{N}\}
with
e_{1}=\mathbf{m}/\sqrt{Nq}
and
e_{2},\dots,e_{N}\in\mathbf{m}^{\perp}
.
In this basis, the coordinates of
\mathbf{m}
are
\sigma_{1}=\sqrt{Nq}
and
\sigma_{r}=0
for
r\geq 2
.
We write

\nabla^{2}H(\mathbf{m})=\begin{pmatrix}A&B^{\top}\\
B&C\end{pmatrix},\qquad A\in\mathbb{R},\quad B\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1},\quad C\in\mathcal{M}_{N-1}(\mathbb{R}).

Recall that if
(X_{1},X_{2})
is a centered Gaussian vector with covariance

\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}\Sigma_{11}&\Sigma_{12}\\
\Sigma_{21}&\Sigma_{22}\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)
, then

(C.8)

\mathrm{Law}(X_{1}\mid X_{2})=\mathcal{N}\!\bigl(\Sigma_{12}\Sigma_{22}^{-1}X_{2},\;\Sigma_{11}-\Sigma_{12}\Sigma_{22}^{-1}\Sigma_{21}\bigr).

The proof relies on the following covariance identities, obtained by differentiating

\mathbb{E}[H(\sigma)H(\tau)]=N\xi(\sigma\cdot\tau/N)
the appropriate number of times and evaluating at
\tau=\sigma=\mathbf{m}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\partial_{ij}H(\mathbf{m})\;\partial_{kl}H(\mathbf{m})\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{\xi^{(4)}(q)}{N^{3}}\,\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\sigma_{k}\sigma_{l}+\frac{\xi^{(3)}(q)}{N^{2}}\bigl(\sigma_{j}\sigma_{k}\delta_{il}+\sigma_{j}\sigma_{l}\delta_{ik}+\sigma_{i}\sigma_{k}\delta_{jl}+\sigma_{i}\sigma_{l}\delta_{jk}\bigr)

(C.9)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}\bigl(\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\bigr),

(C.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\partial_{ij}H(\mathbf{m})\;\partial_{k}H(\mathbf{m})\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{\xi^{(3)}(q)}{N^{2}}\,\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}\sigma_{k}+\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}\bigl(\sigma_{i}\delta_{jk}+\sigma_{j}\delta_{ik}\bigr),

(C.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\partial_{ij}H(\mathbf{m})\;H(\mathbf{m})\bigr]

\displaystyle=\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}\,\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}.

Proof of
(i)
.

For
i,j\in\{2,\dots,N\}
, (
C.11
) and (
C.10
) give

\mathrm{Cov}(C_{ij},H(\mathbf{m}))=0
and
\mathrm{Cov}(C_{ij},\allowbreak\partial_{k}H(\mathbf{m}))=0
for all
k
since
\sigma_{r}=0
for
r\geq 2
.
Hence
C
is independent of
(H(\mathbf{m}),\nabla H(\mathbf{m}))
.

For
i,j,k,l\in\{2,\dots,N\}
, (
C.9
) reduces to

\mathrm{Cov}(C_{ij},C_{kl})=\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}\bigl(\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}+\delta_{il}\delta_{jk}\bigr),

which identifies the law of
C
and yields
(i)
.

Proof of
(ii)
.

For
r\in\{2,\dots,N\}
, the mixed entry is
B_{r}=\partial_{1r}H(\mathbf{m})
.
By (
C.11
),
\mathrm{Cov}(B_{r},H(\mathbf{m}))=0
since
\sigma_{r}=0
.
By (
C.10
) with
(i,j)=(1,r)
,

\mathrm{Cov}(B_{r},\partial_{k}H(\mathbf{m}))=\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}\,\sigma_{1}\,\delta_{rk}=\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\sqrt{\frac{q}{N}}\;\delta_{rk},\qquad k\in\{2,\dots,N\},

and
\mathrm{Cov}(B_{r},\partial_{1}H(\mathbf{m}))=0
.

Next, (
C.9
) with
(i,j,k,l)=(1,r,1,s)
gives, for
r,s\in\{2,\dots,N\}
,

\mathrm{Cov}(B_{r},B_{s})=\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}\delta_{rs}+\frac{\xi^{(3)}(q)}{N^{2}}\sigma_{1}^{2}\,\delta_{rs}=\frac{1}{N}\bigl(\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)+q\,\xi^{(3)}(q)\bigr)\delta_{rs}.

On
\mathbf{m}^{\perp}
, the gradient coordinates have covariance

\mathrm{Var}\bigl((\nabla H(\mathbf{m}))_{\mathbf{m}^{\perp}}\bigr)=\xi^{\prime}(q)\,I_{N-1}
.
Thus Gaussian regression (
C.8
) yields the conditional mean (
C.1
) and

\mathrm{Var}\!\left(B\;\middle|\;H(\mathbf{m}),\nabla H(\mathbf{m})\right)=\frac{1}{N}\bigl(\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)+q\,\xi^{(3)}(q)\bigr)I_{N-1}-\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)^{2}\,q}{N\,\xi^{\prime}(q)}\,I_{N-1},

which is exactly (
C.2
).
Finally,
\mathrm{Cov}(B_{r},C_{kl})=0
for all
r,k,l\geq 2
by (
C.9
), hence
B
is (conditionally) independent of
C
.

Proof of
(iii)
.

Write
g:=\nabla H(\mathbf{m})
and
g_{\parallel}:=\langle g,e_{1}\rangle
.
From (
C.11
) with
i=j=1
,

\mathrm{Cov}(A,H(\mathbf{m}))=\frac{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}\,\sigma_{1}^{2}=\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\,q.

From (
C.10
) with
i=j=k=1
,

\mathrm{Cov}(A,g_{\parallel})=\frac{\xi^{(3)}(q)}{N^{2}}\,\sigma_{1}^{3}+\frac{2\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}\,\sigma_{1}=\bigl(\xi^{(3)}(q)\,q+2\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\bigr)\sqrt{\frac{q}{N}},

and
\mathrm{Cov}(A,\partial_{r}H(\mathbf{m}))=0
for
r\geq 2
.
Hence only
(H(\mathbf{m}),g_{\parallel})
correlates with
A
.

The joint covariance of
(H(\mathbf{m}),g_{\parallel})
is
\Sigma_{X}
in (
C.3
), and the cross-covariance is
\Sigma_{A,X}
in (
C.4
).
Gaussian regression (
C.8
) gives (
C.6
)–(
C.7
).

To compute
\mathrm{Var}(A)
, use (
C.9
) with
i=j=k=l=1
:

\mathrm{Var}(A)=\frac{\xi^{(4)}(q)\,\sigma_{1}^{4}}{N^{3}}+\frac{4\,\xi^{(3)}(q)\,\sigma_{1}^{2}}{N^{2}}+\frac{2\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N}=\frac{\xi^{(4)}(q)\,q^{2}}{N}+\frac{4\,\xi^{(3)}(q)\,q}{N}+\frac{2\,\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}{N},

which is (
C.5
).
Finally,
\mathrm{Cov}(A,C_{kl})=0
for
k,l\geq 2
by (
C.9
), so
A
is independent of
C
.

Proof of
(iv)
.

The vector
\bigl(A,B,C,H(\mathbf{m}),\nabla H(\mathbf{m})\bigr)
is jointly Gaussian, hence so is the conditional law of
(A,B,C)
given
(H(\mathbf{m}),\nabla H(\mathbf{m}))
.
Parts
(i)
–
(iii)
show that
C
is independent of
(A,B,H(\mathbf{m}),\nabla H(\mathbf{m}))
.

Moreover, by (
C.9
),
\mathrm{Cov}(A,B_{r})=0
for all
r\geq 2
,
and by (
C.10
),

\mathrm{Cov}(A,(\nabla H(\mathbf{m}))_{\mathbf{m}^{\perp}})=0
while

\mathrm{Cov}(B,(H(\mathbf{m}),g_{\parallel}))=0
.
Since
(H(\mathbf{m}),g_{\parallel})
is independent of
(\nabla H(\mathbf{m}))_{\mathbf{m}^{\perp}}
, it follows that,
conditionally on
(H(\mathbf{m}),\nabla H(\mathbf{m}))
, the variables
A
and
B
are independent as well.
This proves
(iv)
.
∎

C.2.
Free convolution with a semicircle law

In this subsection, we collect standard results on the free convolution of a probability measure with a semicircle law.

Let
\mu
be a probability measure on
\mathbb{R}
and let
t>0
. Denote by
\sigma_{t}
the semicircle law of variance
t
. For every
u\in\mathbb{R}
, define

(C.12)

v_{t}(u)=\inf\Bigl\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{+}:\int\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda)}{|\lambda-u|^{2}+v^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{t}\Bigr\},

the domain

(C.13)

\Omega_{\mu,t}=\bigl\{u+iv\in\mathbb{C}^{+}:v>v_{t}(u)\bigr\},

and the mapping

(C.14)

H_{t}\colon\overline{\Omega_{\mu,t}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{+}\cup\mathbb{R},\qquad z\mapsto z+tG_{\mu}(z).

The following lemma, due to Biane
[
8
]
, describes the analytic properties of
H_{t}
and the subordination phenomenon for free convolution.

Lemma C.2

(Biane
[
8
]
)
.

Let
\mu
be a probability measure on
\mathbb{R}
and
t>0
. The mapping
H_{t}
is a homeomorphism from
\overline{\Omega_{\mu,t}}
onto
\mathbb{C}^{+}\cup\mathbb{R}
, which restricts to a conformal bijection from
\Omega_{\mu,t}
onto
\mathbb{C}^{+}
. Its inverse

\omega_{\mu,t}\colon\mathbb{C}^{+}\cup\mathbb{R}\longrightarrow\overline{\Omega_{\mu,t}}

is called the
subordination function
. Moreover, for every
z\in\Omega_{\mu,t}
,

(C.15)

G_{\mu\boxplus\sigma_{t}}\bigl(z+tG_{\mu}(z)\bigr)=G_{\mu}(z).

If the support of
\mu
is bounded from below, the left edge of
\mu\boxplus\sigma_{t}
is given by
\ell(\mu\boxplus\sigma_{t})=H_{t}(\omega)
, where

(C.16)

\omega=\inf\Bigl\{\omega\in\mathbb{R}:\int\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda)}{(\lambda-\omega)^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{t}\Bigr\}.

Lemma
C.2
admits a natural reformulation in the language of PDEs. Writing
\mu_{t}:=\mu\boxplus\sigma_{t}
, the Stieltjes transform
G_{\mu_{t}}
satisfies the complex Burgers equation: for every
z
outside the support of
\mu_{t}
,

(C.17)

\partial_{t}G_{\mu_{t}}(z)+G_{\mu_{t}}(z)\,\partial_{z}G_{\mu_{t}}(z)=0.

This equation can be solved by the method of characteristics. The characteristic emanating from
z\in\mathbb{C}^{+}
is the straight line
t\mapsto z+tG_{\mu}(z)
, and (
C.15
) expresses the fact that the solution is constant along characteristics (for suitable
z
). For
\omega\in(-\infty,\ell(\mu)]
, the map
z\mapsto z+tG_{\mu}(z)
is injective on
(-\infty,\omega)
provided

\int\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda)}{(\lambda-\omega)^{2}}<\frac{1}{t}.

In view of (
C.16
), the left edge of
\mu_{t}
can therefore be interpreted as the first point at which the solution develops a shock at time
t
.

The PDE interpretation makes it possible to compute the logarithmic potential of
\mu\boxplus\sigma_{t}
via the Hopf–Lax formula.

Lemma C.3

(Hopf–Lax formula for the logarithmic potential)
.

Let
\mu
be a probability measure on
\mathbb{R}
with support bounded from below and let
t>0
.

(1)

Let
x\in\mathbb{R}
and set
\omega:=\omega_{\mu,t}(x)\in\mathbb{C}^{+}\cup\mathbb{R}
. Define the characteristic path
(z_{s})_{s\in[0,t]}
by

(C.18)

z_{s}=\omega+s\,G_{\mu}(\omega).

If
x\leq\ell(\mu\boxplus\sigma_{t})
, then for every
s\in[0,t]
,

(C.19)

x\leq z_{s}\leq\ell(\mu\boxplus\sigma_{s}).

If instead
x
lies in the interior of
\mathrm{supp}(\mu\boxplus\sigma_{t})
, then for every
s\in[0,t)
,

\Im\bigl(\omega+s\,G_{\mu}(\omega)\bigr)>0.

(2)

For every
x\leq\ell(\mu\boxplus\sigma_{t})
,

(C.20)

\int\log|\lambda-x|\,\mathrm{d}(\mu\boxplus\sigma_{t})(\lambda)=\int\log|\lambda-\omega_{\mu,t}(x)|\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda)+\frac{\Re\bigl(\omega_{\mu,t}(x)-x\bigr)^{2}-\Im\bigl(\omega_{\mu,t}(x)\bigr)^{2}}{2t}\\
=\inf_{u\leq\ell(\mu)}\;\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\geq 0\\
u+iv\in\overline{\Omega_{\mu,t}}\end{subarray}}\Bigl(\int\log|\lambda-(u+iv)|\,\mathrm{d}\mu(\lambda)+\frac{(x-u)^{2}-v^{2}}{2t}\Bigr).

Moreover, the infimum in (
C.20
) is uniquely attained at
(u,v)=\bigl(\Re(\omega_{\mu,t}(x)),\,\Im(\omega_{\mu,t}(x))\bigr)
.

Proof.

See, e.g.,
[
9
, Lemma A.3]
.
∎

C.3.
Determinant asymptotics

In this subsection, we compute the conditional expectation of
|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))|
given the gradient and value of the TAP free energy.

Lemma C.4
.

Fix
q\in(0,1)
,
\delta\in(0,1)
, and let
\zeta\in\mathcal{P}([0,1])
.
For every
s\in[0,1]
, set

\pi_{s}\zeta:=\zeta|_{(s,1]}+\zeta([0,s])\delta_{s},\qquad T_{\zeta,s}(m):=\partial_{mm}h_{\pi_{s}\zeta}(s,m)+\xi^{\prime\prime}(s)\int_{s}^{1}\pi_{s}\zeta([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t,\qquad m\in[-1,1].

Assume that there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
u_{0}>0
such that

[q-\varepsilon_{0},q+\varepsilon_{0}]\subset(0,1)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\zeta([0,s])\geq u_{0}\quad\text{for every }s\in[q-\varepsilon_{0},q+\varepsilon_{0}].

Let
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
, let
\mathbf{m}\in[-1+\delta,1-\delta]^{N}
satisfy

q_{\mathbf{m}}\in(q-\varepsilon,q+\varepsilon),\qquad\mathrm{dist}\!\bigl(\zeta_{\mathbf{m}},\pi_{q}\zeta\bigr)\leq\varepsilon,

and let
f\in\mathbb{R}
. Writing
T_{\zeta}:=T_{\zeta,q}
, one has

(C.21)

\log\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))\bigr|\;\Bigm|\;\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,\,F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf\Bigr]\\
=N\int\log|x|\,\mathrm{d}\Bigl((T_{\zeta}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})})\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}\Bigr)(x)+O\!\left(\left(\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta}\right)N\right)+o(N).

The implicit constant in the
O(\cdot)
term depends only on
\xi
,
f
,
u_{0}
, and
\varepsilon_{0}
; the
o(N)
term is the error from the deformed GOE asymptotics and is uniform over
\mathbf{m}
satisfying the stated constraints.

Proof.

Set
\bar{\zeta}:=\pi_{q_{\mathbf{m}}}\zeta
.
Since only the values of the cumulative distribution function on
[q_{\mathbf{m}},1]
enter the
definition of
F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})
, we have

F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=F_{\mathrm{TAP},\bar{\zeta}}(\mathbf{m})
.
Moreover, a direct inspection of the distribution functions shows that

\mathrm{dist}(\bar{\zeta},\pi_{q}\zeta)\leq C|q_{\mathbf{m}}-q|\leq C\varepsilon
,
hence

(C.22)

\mathrm{dist}(\bar{\zeta},\zeta_{\mathbf{m}})\leq C\varepsilon.

By assumption,

\bar{\zeta}(\{q_{\mathbf{m}}\})=\zeta([0,q_{\mathbf{m}}])\geq u_{0}
.
Since
\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}
is supported in
[-1+\delta,1-\delta]
, Lemma
B.7

and (
C.22
) imply

(C.23)

\bigl|\Delta_{\bar{\zeta}}^{\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}\bigr|\leq C\,\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta}.

Write
G(\sigma):=N\mathrm{TAP}(\mu_{N}^{(\sigma)},\bar{\zeta})
.
Since
F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\sigma)
depends on
\zeta
only through
\zeta([0,t])
for
t\in[q_{\sigma},1]
, and
\bar{\zeta}
agrees with
\zeta
on
[q_{\mathbf{m}},1]
, we have

F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=H(\mathbf{m})+G(\mathbf{m})
.
Since
G
is deterministic, the event

\{\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,\;F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf\}

determines
\nabla H(\mathbf{m})
and
H(\mathbf{m})
as deterministic functions of
\mathbf{m}
.

Step 1: deterministic Hessian.

Writing
q_{\sigma}=\|\sigma\|^{2}/N
, the chain rule gives

\partial_{i}G(\sigma)=-\partial_{m}h_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\sigma},\sigma_{i})-\frac{2\sigma_{i}}{N}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{N}\partial_{q}h_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\sigma},\sigma_{k})+N\partial_{q}\mathcal{U}_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\sigma})\right).

Since
\bar{\zeta}
agrees with
\zeta
on
(q_{\mathbf{m}},1]
and the Parisi PDE on
[q_{\sigma},1]
depends only on
\zeta|_{[q_{\sigma},1]}
, the functions
h_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\sigma},\cdot)
and
h_{\zeta}(q_{\sigma},\cdot)
coincide for
q_{\sigma}\geq q_{\mathbf{m}}
; their
m
-derivatives and right
q
-derivatives at
q_{\sigma}=q_{\mathbf{m}}
also agree. In (
C.24
) below, all
q
-derivatives of
h_{\bar{\zeta}}
at
q=q_{\mathbf{m}}
are right derivatives (cf. the convention stated in the proof of Lemma
B.8
); when
\bar{\zeta}
has an atom at
q_{\mathbf{m}}
, the one-sided derivatives
\partial_{q}
,
\partial_{qm}
, and
\partial_{qq}
remain bounded and the corresponding terms are absorbed into the remainder
R_{N}
in (
C.25
). Differentiating once more at
\sigma=\mathbf{m}
yields

(C.24)

\partial_{ij}G(\mathbf{m})=-\delta_{ij}\,\partial_{mm}h_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{i})-\frac{\delta_{ij}}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}2\partial_{q}h_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{k})-2\delta_{ij}\,\partial_{q}\mathcal{U}_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\mathbf{m}})\\
-\frac{2m_{j}}{N}\partial_{qm}h_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{i})-\frac{2m_{i}}{N}\partial_{qm}h_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{j})\\
-\frac{4m_{i}m_{j}}{N}\left(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\partial_{qq}h_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m_{k})+\partial_{qq}\mathcal{U}_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\mathbf{m}})\right).

By the exact defect formula (
B.34
), the diagonal terms in the
first line combine as

-\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}(m_{i})
, where

\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}(m):=\partial_{mm}h_{\bar{\zeta}}(q_{\mathbf{m}},m)+\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})\left(\int_{q_{\mathbf{m}}}^{1}\bar{\zeta}([0,t])\,\mathrm{d}t-\Delta_{\bar{\zeta}}^{\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}}\right).

The remaining cross terms form a deterministic, symmetric matrix
R_{N}
with
entries
O(1/N)
uniformly in
\mathbf{m}
, so that

(C.25)

\nabla^{2}G(\mathbf{m})=-\mathrm{diag}\Bigl(\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}(m_{1}),\ldots,\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}(m_{N})\Bigr)+R_{N},

(C.26)

\|R_{N}\|_{\mathrm{HS}}=O(1).

(The uniform
O(1/N)
bound follows from (
C.24
) and the
uniform boundedness of the mixed derivatives of
h_{\bar{\zeta}}
on

[q-\varepsilon_{0},q+\varepsilon_{0}]\times[-1+\delta,1-\delta]
.)

Step 2: conditional law of the random Hessian.

Choose an orthonormal basis

e_{1}=\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\sqrt{Nq_{\mathbf{m}}}},\qquad e_{2},\dots,e_{N}\in\mathbf{m}^{\perp},

and let
U=(e_{1}|\cdots|e_{N})
.
By Lemma
C.1
, conditionally on

\{\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,\;F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf\}
,
the Hessian of
H
in this basis has the form

U^{\top}\nabla^{2}H(\mathbf{m})\,U=\begin{pmatrix}A&B^{\top}\\
B&C\end{pmatrix},

where
C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}\,\mathrm{GOE}_{N-1}/\sqrt{N}
, and
C
is independent of
(A,B)
.
Let

\widehat{W}_{N}:=\begin{pmatrix}\hat{a}&\hat{b}^{\top}\\
\hat{b}&C\end{pmatrix},

where
\hat{a}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,2\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})/N)
and

\hat{b}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})I_{N-1}/N)
are independent of
C
.
Then

\widehat{W}_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}\,\mathrm{GOE}_{N}/\sqrt{N}
.
Define

K_{N}:=\begin{pmatrix}A-\hat{a}&(B-\hat{b})^{\top}\\
B-\hat{b}&0\end{pmatrix}.

By construction,

(C.27)

U^{\top}\nabla^{2}H(\mathbf{m})\,U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\widehat{W}_{N}+K_{N}.

Moreover,
\mathrm{rank}(K_{N})\leq 2
, and since
f
is fixed and
(A,B)
are conditionally
Gaussian with bounded means and variances,
K_{N}
has uniformly bounded
sub-exponential moments.

Set
D_{N}:=\mathrm{diag}\bigl(\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}(m_{1}),\ldots,\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}(m_{N})\bigr)
.
Combining (
C.25
), (
C.26
), and (
C.27
), we obtain
that conditionally on

\{\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,\;F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf\}
,

U^{\top}\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})\,U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\widehat{W}_{N}-U^{\top}D_{N}U+K_{N}+U^{\top}R_{N}U,

where
K_{N}+U^{\top}R_{N}U
is the sum of a rank-
2
random matrix and a deterministic
matrix with Hilbert–Schmidt norm
O(1)
.
By orthogonal invariance of the GOE (
U\widehat{W}_{N}U^{\top}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\widehat{W}_{N}
),

\det(\widehat{W}_{N}-U^{\top}D_{N}U)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}\det\!\left(\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}\,\frac{\mathrm{GOE}_{N}}{\sqrt{N}}-D_{N}\right).

A standard truncation of the logarithm near
0
, together with finite-rank
interlacing, stability under bounded Hilbert–Schmidt perturbations, and the usual
least-singular-value bound for deformed GOE matrices, therefore gives

(C.28)

\log\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\bigl|\det(\nabla^{2}F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m}))\bigr|\;\Bigm|\;\nabla F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=0,\;F_{\mathrm{TAP},\zeta}(\mathbf{m})=Nf\Bigr]\\
=\log\mathbb{E}\left|\det\!\left(\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}\,\frac{\mathrm{GOE}_{N}}{\sqrt{N}}-D_{N}\right)\right|+o(N).

Since
\mathrm{GOE}_{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}-\mathrm{GOE}_{N}
and the determinant is taken in absolute value,
the right-hand side is also

(C.29)

\log\mathbb{E}\left|\det\!\left(\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}\,\frac{\mathrm{GOE}_{N}}{\sqrt{N}}+D_{N}\right)\right|+o(N).

Step 3: deformed GOE asymptotics.

The entries of
D_{N}
stay in a deterministic compact interval depending only on

\xi
,
u_{0}
,
\varepsilon_{0}
, and
\delta
.
For such deformed GOE ensembles, the empirical spectral measure converges to

(\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})})\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}
,
and the logarithmic linear statistic

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\log|\lambda_{i}|
is stable on the
o(N)
scale after the same
truncation/least-singular-value argument.
The
o(N)
error is uniform over diagonal matrices
D_{N}
in any fixed compact set bounded away from
0
; since the entries of
D_{N}
lie in such a compact set (depending only on
\xi
,
u_{0}
,
\varepsilon_{0}
,
\delta
), this error is
o(N)
with an implicit rate independent of
\mathbf{m}
. Thus

(C.30)

\log\mathbb{E}\left|\det\!\left(\sqrt{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}\,\frac{\mathrm{GOE}_{N}}{\sqrt{N}}+D_{N}\right)\right|=N\int\log|x|\,\mathrm{d}\bigl((\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})})\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}\bigr)(x)+o(N).

Step 4: comparison with the target measure.

By (
C.23
), the continuity of
s\mapsto\pi_{s}\zeta
in the metric

\mathrm{dist}
, the continuity of
(s,\eta,m)\mapsto\partial_{mm}h_{\eta}(s,m)
on compact
subsets of
(0,1)\times\mathcal{P}([0,1])\times(-1,1)
, and the fact that
|q_{\mathbf{m}}-q|\leq\varepsilon
,
we have

\sup_{m\in[-1+\delta,1-\delta]}\bigl|\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}(m)-T_{\zeta,q}(m)\bigr|\leq C\left(\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta}\right).

Hence

(C.31)

\mathrm{dist}\!\bigl(\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})},\,T_{\zeta}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})}\bigr)\leq C\left(\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta}\right),\qquad\bigl|\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})-\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)\bigr|\leq C\varepsilon.

All measures involved are supported in a common compact interval, and free
convolution with a semicircle law yields a bounded density on this compact family
(the free convolution
\nu\boxplus\sigma_{t}
has a density bounded by
1/(\pi t)
, so
\log|x|
is integrable uniformly over the family; together with weak continuity of
\nu\mapsto\nu\boxplus\sigma_{t}
, this yields the claimed uniform continuity by dominated convergence).
Therefore the map

(\nu,t)\mapsto\int\log|x|\,\mathrm{d}(\nu\boxplus\sigma_{t})(x)

is uniformly continuous on this family. Using (
C.31
), we get

\int\log|x|\,\mathrm{d}\bigl((\widehat{T}_{\mathbf{m},\zeta}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})})\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q_{\mathbf{m}})}\bigr)(x)=\int\log|x|\,\mathrm{d}\Bigl((T_{\zeta}\#\mu_{N}^{(\mathbf{m})})\boxplus\sigma_{\xi^{\prime\prime}(q)}\Bigr)(x)+O\!\left(\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon}{\delta}\right).

Combining this with (
C.28
), (
C.29
), and
(
C.30
) proves (
C.21
).
∎
